# INIT Command - Initialization & Memory Bank Setup

Initializes the Memory Bank system for the project. This command sets up the knowledge base that powers all other workflow commands.

**This command does NOT:**
- Create tasks (use `/banyan-task` for Level 1 tasks)
- Evaluate complexity (happens in `/banyan-task` or `/banyan-roadmap`)
- Route to next workflow (user chooses next command)

---

## Memory Bank Integration

**Creates/Updates:**
- `CLAUDE.md` - **Project guidance for Claude Code** (Banyan workflow configuration)
- `memory-bank/projectbrief.md` - Project overview and repository structure
- `memory-bank/techContext.md` - Technology stack and development commands
- `memory-bank/systemPatterns.md` - Architecture patterns and conventions
- `memory-bank/productBrief.md` - Product context (personas, NFRs, integrations)
- `memory-bank/tasks.md` - Task registry table (if not exists)
- `memory-bank/tasks/` - Directory for per-task files (created empty)
- `memory-bank/progress.md` - Progress tracking (initialized)
- `memory-bank/projectConfig.md` - Plugin version tracking and project configuration
- `memory-bank/agent-rules/_learned/` - Directory for auto-generated learned rules (continuous learning)
- `memory-bank/learning-log.md` - Continuous learning event ledger
- `memory-bank/learning-metrics.md` - Learning system configuration and metrics
- `.agent-logs/` - Session logging scripts (copied from plugin)
- `.claude/settings.local.json` - Hook configuration for session logging

---

## Workflow

### Step 1: Verify Memory Bank Exists

Check if `memory-bank/` directory and core files exist.

**If memory-bank/ does NOT exist or is empty:**
- Create the directory structure
- Proceed to **Step 1.1: Brownfield Detection** (full discovery)

**If memory-bank/ exists with populated files:**
- Proceed to **Step 1.2: Memory Bank Refresh** (update existing files)

```
memory-bank/
├── tasks.md          ← task registry table
├── tasks/            ← per-task plan files
├── progress.md
├── projectbrief.md
├── techContext.md
├── systemPatterns.md
├── productBrief.md
└── (other files as needed)
```

### Step 1.5: Git Environment Detection

**Purpose**: Detect git repository and provider information for automated git workflow integration.

**Note**: This step runs for BOTH greenfield and brownfield projects after their respective memory bank setup.

1. **Check if git repository**
   ```bash
   git rev-parse --git-dir 2>/dev/null
   ```
   - If successful: This is a git repository
   - If fails: Not a git repository (skip git workflow features)

2. **Detect git hosting provider** (only if git repo exists)

   a. **Check CLI tools availability:**
   ```bash
   which gh    # GitHub CLI
   which glab  # GitLab CLI
   which bb    # Bitbucket CLI (if exists)
   ```

   b. **Parse remote URL:**
   ```bash
   git remote get-url origin 2>/dev/null
   ```

   c. **Map to provider:**
   - Contains `github.com` → GitHub
   - Contains `gitlab.com` or `gitlab.` → GitLab
   - Contains `bitbucket.org` → Bitbucket
   - Other/None → Unknown or Local-only

3. **Detect default branch**
   ```bash
   git symbolic-ref refs/remotes/origin/HEAD 2>/dev/null | sed 's@^refs/remotes/origin/@@'
   ```
   - Common values: `main`, `master`, `develop`
   - If no remote: Use current branch or `main`

4. **Determine Archive Strategy** (only if git repo)

   Ask the user how they want completed tasks handled:
   ```
   How should completed tasks be delivered?

     1. Push & PR (Recommended for teams)
        Push the feature branch to remote and create a pull request.
        The branch is NOT merged locally - the PR process handles merging.

     2. Local Merge
        Merge the feature branch into main locally.
        No push to remote, no PR creation.

   How would you like to proceed? [1/2]:
   ```

   - If user selects 1: Archive Strategy = `push-and-pr`
   - If user selects 2: Archive Strategy = `local-merge`
   - If no remote URL detected: Default to `local-merge` (skip prompt)

5. **Update projectbrief.md**

   Add or update the **Git Configuration** section:
   ```markdown
   ## Git Configuration
   - **Repository**: Yes/No
   - **Provider**: GitHub/GitLab/Bitbucket/Unknown/None
   - **CLI Available**: gh/glab/bb/none
   - **Remote URL**: [url or "none"]
   - **Default Branch**: [main/master/other]
   - **Archive Strategy**: push-and-pr / local-merge
   ```

6. **Check current branch status** (if git repo)
   ```bash
   git status --porcelain
   ```

   If working directory has uncommitted changes:
   - Display warning to user:
     ```
     ⚠️  Git Status Warning
     Working directory has uncommitted changes. These should be committed or stashed before starting new work.
     ```

### Step 4: CLAUDE.md Setup (IMPORTANT)

**Purpose**: Install or update the project's `CLAUDE.md` file with Banyan Memory Bank guidance.

#### Why CLAUDE.md Matters

The `CLAUDE.md` file is **critical** for proper Banyan workflow operation:

1. **Provides Project Context to Claude Code** - When Claude Code opens a project, it reads `CLAUDE.md` to understand how to work with the codebase
2. **Documents the Memory Bank System** - Explains where task tracking, context, and progress files are located
3. **Defines Complexity Levels & Workflows** - Guides Claude through the correct workflow for each task type
4. **Establishes Standards** - 12-Factor App, Observability, API conventions
5. **Enables Interruption Recovery** - Explains how state tracking and resumption work
6. **Configures Model Selection** - Documents which models to use for which operations

**Without a properly configured CLAUDE.md:**
- Claude Code won't know about the Memory Bank system
- Workflows like `/banyan-build` may not function correctly
- Context files won't be loaded properly
- Quality standards won't be enforced

---

#### Step 4.1: Check for Existing CLAUDE.md

```bash
ls CLAUDE.md 2>/dev/null
```

**CASE A: No CLAUDE.md exists** → Proceed to Step 4.2 (Fresh Install)

**CASE B: CLAUDE.md exists** → Proceed to Step 4.3 (Merge/Update)

---

#### Step 4.2: Fresh Install (No Existing CLAUDE.md)

When no `CLAUDE.md` exists, copy the template from the plugin:

```bash
cp "/home/sahil/Downloads/banyan-memory-bank//templates/CLAUDE-template.md" ./CLAUDE.md
```

Review and customize CLAUDE.md as needed for your project-specific requirements.
```

Proceed to Step 5.

grep -l "banyan-" CLAUDE.md 2>/dev/null

2. Or manually merge from: /home/sahil/Downloads/banyan-memory-bank//templates/CLAUDE-template.md
```
Skip to Step 5.

**If NO Banyan content exists:**

**Step 4.3.2: Present Merge Options**

```

Your project has an existing CLAUDE.md file, but it doesn't contain

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

OPTIONS:

  1. APPEND Banyan configuration (Recommended)
     Keep your existing content and add Banyan sections at the end.

  2. PREPEND Banyan configuration
     Add Banyan sections at the beginning, followed by your content.

How would you like to proceed? [1/2/3/4]:
```

## Usage

```
/banyan-init
```

No arguments needed. The command will:
1. Detect and analyze the codebase (brownfield vs greenfield)
2. Create or refresh the Memory Bank files
3. Set up git integration (detect provider, CLI tools)
4. Configure session logging hooks (if not already set up)
5. **Install or merge CLAUDE.md** (critical for workflow operation)
6. Display summary and next steps

---

## When to Run

- **First time on a project**: Creates full Memory Bank
- **After significant codebase changes**: Refreshes Memory Bank
- **Starting fresh session**: Verifies Memory Bank is current
- **User requests refresh**: Forces full refresh

---

## Next Steps After Init

| Scenario | Command |
|----------|---------|
| Quick bug fix or typo | `/banyan-task Fix the typo in README` |
| Simple Level 1 task | `/banyan-task [task description]` |
| New feature (Level 2+) | `/banyan-roadmap feature create [name]` |
| View roadmap | `/banyan-roadmap view` |
| Existing task to continue | Check `memory-bank/tasks.md` |

1	# Brownfield Detection & Memory Bank Bootstrap
2	
3	**Purpose**: Detect if this is an existing codebase ("brownfield") vs a new/starter project ("greenfield"), and if brownfield, launch explorer sub-agents to properly populate the memory bank.
4	
5	#### 1.1.1: Assess Codebase Maturity
6	
7	Run the following checks to determine if this is a meaningful existing codebase:
8	
9	```bash
10	# Count source files (excluding node_modules, vendor, etc.)
11	find . -type f \( -name "*.ts" -o -name "*.tsx" -o -name "*.js" -o -name "*.jsx" -o -name "*.py" -o -name "*.go" -o -name "*.rs" -o -name "*.java" -o -name "*.rb" -o -name "*.php" -o -name "*.swift" -o -name "*.kt" \) \
12	  -not -path "*/node_modules/*" -not -path "*/.git/*" -not -path "*/vendor/*" -not -path "*/dist/*" -not -path "*/build/*" -not -path "*/__pycache__/*" \
13	  2>/dev/null | wc -l
14	
15	# Check for package manifests
16	ls package.json pyproject.toml requirements.txt Cargo.toml go.mod pom.xml build.gradle Gemfile composer.json Package.swift 2>/dev/null
17	
18	# Check git history depth (if git repo)
19	git rev-list --count HEAD 2>/dev/null
20	
21	# Check for configuration files indicating mature project
22	ls .env.example docker-compose.yml Dockerfile Makefile tsconfig.json .eslintrc* .prettierrc* jest.config.* vitest.config.* 2>/dev/null
23	```
24	
25	#### 1.1.2: Repository Structure Detection (Mono-repo vs Poly-repo)
26	
27	**Purpose**: Determine if this is a mono-repo (multiple services/apps in one repository) or a poly-repo (single service/app per repository).
28	
29	**Detection Checks:**
30	
31	```bash
32	# Check for workspace configuration files
33	ls pnpm-workspace.yaml lerna.json nx.json turbo.json rush.json 2>/dev/null
34	
35	# Check package.json for workspaces field
36	grep -l '"workspaces"' package.json 2>/dev/null
37	
38	# Check for Cargo workspace
39	grep -l '\[workspace\]' Cargo.toml 2>/dev/null
40	
41	# Check for Go workspace
42	ls go.work 2>/dev/null
43	
44	# Count package manifests in subdirectories
45	find . -name "package.json" -not -path "*/node_modules/*" -not -path "*/.git/*" 2>/dev/null | wc -l
46	
47	# Check for common mono-repo directory patterns
48	ls -d apps packages services libs modules projects 2>/dev/null
49	```
50	
51	**Classification Criteria:**
52	
53	**MONO-REPO** indicators (if ANY of these are true):
54	- Workspace config exists (pnpm-workspace.yaml, lerna.json, nx.json, turbo.json, rush.json)
55	- `package.json` contains `"workspaces"` field
56	- `Cargo.toml` contains `[workspace]` section
57	- `go.work` file exists (Go workspaces)
58	- Multiple `package.json` files in subdirectories (3+)
59	- Standard mono-repo directories exist (`apps/`, `packages/`, `services/`, `libs/`)
60	
61	**POLY-REPO** indicators (default if none of the above):
62	- Single package manifest at root only
63	- No workspace configuration
64	- Single app/service structure
65	
66	**Mono-repo Sub-classification:**
67	
68	| Pattern | Tool/Convention | Characteristics |
69	|---------|-----------------|-----------------|
70	| `nx.json` | Nx | Task orchestration, affected commands, caching |
71	| `turbo.json` | Turborepo | Pipeline-based builds, remote caching |
72	| `lerna.json` | Lerna | Package publishing, versioning |
73	| `pnpm-workspace.yaml` | pnpm workspaces | Native pnpm workspace support |
74	| `rush.json` | Rush | Enterprise mono-repo management |
75	| `go.work` | Go workspaces | Multi-module Go development |
76	| `[workspace]` in Cargo.toml | Cargo workspaces | Rust multi-crate projects |
77	| Directory convention only | Custom | Manual workspace management |
78	
79	**Record in `memory-bank/projectbrief.md`:**
80	
81	```markdown
82	## Repository Structure
83	- **Type**: Mono-repo / Poly-repo
84	- **Workspace Tool**: [Nx/Turborepo/Lerna/pnpm/Rush/Cargo/Go/Custom/None]
85	- **Workspace Root**: [path or N/A]
86	- **Apps/Services**: [list of app directories if mono-repo]
87	- **Shared Packages**: [list of shared package directories if mono-repo]
88	```
89	
90	#### 1.1.3: Classification Criteria
91	
92	**GREENFIELD** (simple starter repo) - Skip to Step 1.3:
93	- Less than 10 source files, OR
94	- Less than 5 git commits, OR
95	- Only contains boilerplate/scaffold files (README, single config, etc.)
96	
97	**BROWNFIELD** (meaningful existing codebase) - Proceed to Step 1.1.4 (explorer agents):
98	- 10+ source files, AND
99	- Multiple directories with source code, AND
100	- Evidence of established patterns (tests, configs, multiple modules)
101	
102	#### 1.1.4: Launch Explorer Sub-Agents (Brownfield Only)
103	
104	If brownfield detected, launch **parallel explorer sub-agents** to discover the codebase. Use the Task tool with `subagent_type: "Explore"`.
105	
106	**Launch these agents IN PARALLEL:**
107	
108	**Agent 1: Tech Stack Discovery**
109	```
110	Prompt: "Analyze this codebase to identify the complete technology stack. Find:
111	1. Primary programming language(s) and versions
112	2. Frameworks (web, API, testing, etc.)
113	3. Key dependencies/libraries and their purposes
114	4. Build tools and package managers
115	5. Database/storage technologies
116	6. Infrastructure tools (Docker, K8s, etc.)
117	7. CI/CD configuration
118	
119	Look at: package.json, requirements.txt, Cargo.toml, go.mod, config files,
120	Dockerfiles, CI configs (.github/workflows, .gitlab-ci.yml, etc.)
121	
122	Output a structured summary suitable for memory-bank/techContext.md"
123	```
124	
125	**Agent 2: Project Structure & Architecture**
126	```
127	Prompt: "Analyze the project structure and architecture patterns. Discover:
128	1. Directory structure and organization pattern (feature-based, layer-based, etc.)
129	2. Entry points (main files, index files, app bootstrap)
130	3. Core modules/components and their responsibilities
131	4. API structure (routes, controllers, handlers)
132	5. Data layer (models, repositories, schemas)
133	6. Shared utilities and helpers
134	7. Testing Patterns - Analyze existing tests to document:
135	   a. Test location: co-located with source vs separate directory (tests/, __tests__/)
136	   b. File mapping: one test file per module/component vs per source file vs feature-based
137	   c. Naming convention: *.test.ts, *.spec.ts, test_*.py, *_test.go, etc.
138	   d. Within-file grouping: by behavior/scenario vs by method/function
139	   e. Describe/context nesting depth: shallow (1 level) vs deep
140	   f. Setup sharing: shared fixtures, per-test setup, factory functions
141	   g. Framework and assertion style
142	   h. Mocking approach: jest.mock, dependency injection, test doubles, etc.
143	   i. Test scope emphasis: unit-heavy vs integration-heavy vs E2E-first
144	   j. Typical test-to-source ratio (count test files vs source files in a representative module)
145	   k. What is NOT tested: identify patterns the project deliberately skips
146	   Output these as a 'Testing Patterns' section with the subsections above filled in.
147	8. Guiding Principles - Identify foundational architectural constraints from:
148	   - README, CLAUDE.md, or other project docs mentioning compliance, standards, patterns
149	   - Existing code patterns that reveal architectural decisions (e.g., all traffic through a gateway,
150	     all data encrypted, specific naming conventions enforced by linters)
151	   - Common industry principles relevant to the project type
152	   Output these as a 'Guiding Principles' table with Principle name and Description.
153	
154	Map the high-level architecture. Output suitable for memory-bank/systemPatterns.md
155	(include a Guiding Principles section at the top and a Testing Patterns section)"
156	```
157	
158	**Agent 3: Development Workflow Discovery** (if sufficient config files exist)
159	```
160	Prompt: "Discover the development workflow and conventions. Find:
161	1. Available npm/make/cargo scripts and commands
162	2. Testing commands and frameworks
163	3. Linting and formatting setup
164	4. Environment configuration approach
165	5. Local development setup requirements
166	6. Coding conventions (from config files)
167	
168	Output development commands and setup suitable for memory-bank/techContext.md"

170	
171	**Agent 4: Mono-repo Workspace Discovery** (ONLY if mono-repo detected in Step 1.1.2)

173	Prompt: "This is a mono-repo. Analyze the workspace structure in detail:
174	1. List all apps/services with their purposes (check apps/, services/, projects/)
175	2. List all shared packages/libraries (check packages/, libs/, modules/)
176	3. Map dependencies between packages (which apps use which shared packages)
177	4. Identify the workspace tool and its configuration (nx.json, turbo.json, etc.)
178	5. Document workspace-specific commands (nx run, turbo run, pnpm -r, etc.)
179	6. Find any workspace-level scripts or orchestration
180	7. Identify build order or dependency graph if defined
181	8. Check for shared configurations (tsconfig.base.json, shared eslint, etc.)
182	
183	Output:
184	- Complete list of workspace members with descriptions
185	- Dependency graph between packages
186	- Workspace-aware commands for building, testing, and running
187	- Any shared configuration patterns
188	
189	This output will be used to populate the Repository Structure section of projectbrief.md
190	and workspace-specific commands in techContext.md"

213	   - Availability (uptime targets, DR, RTO/RPO)

216	   - Internationalization (languages, localization)
217	9. Integration Points - External systems, APIs consumed/provided
218	10. Constraints & Assumptions - Technical/business constraints
219	11. Risks - Any documented risks
220	
221	Look at: README files, documentation, marketing copy, user-facing strings,
222	configuration options, test scenarios, API documentation, error messages,
223	permission/role systems, compliance-related code, performance tests,

1	# CLAUDE.md

3	This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

5	## Memory Bank System
6	
7	This project uses the **Banyan Memory Bank** system for structured development and task management. All Memory Bank files are located in the `memory-bank/` directory at the project root.
8	
9	### Core Memory Bank Files
10	
11	- **`memory-bank/tasks.md`** - Task registry: at-a-glance table of all tasks with phase and status
12	- **`memory-bank/tasks/TASK-XXX.md`** - Per-task file: full plan, user journey, implementation roadmap, and live execution state for one task
13	- **`memory-bank/progress.md`** - Implementation status and phase completion tracking (only updated by the banyan-archive command)
14	- **`memory-bank/projectConfig.md`** - Plugin version tracking and project configuration (auto-managed by `/banyan-init`)
15	- **`memory-bank/projectbrief.md`** - Project foundation, objectives, and repository structure
16	- **`memory-bank/productBrief.md`** - Product context: key functionality, markets, personas, NFRs, and integrations
17	- **`memory-bank/techContext.md`** - Technology stack, infrastructure, component structure, and development commands
18	- **`memory-bank/systemPatterns.md`** - System architecture patterns
19	- **`memory-bank/roadmap.md`** - Product roadmap with versions, features, and release tracking (required for Level 2-4 tasks)
20	- **`memory-bank/creative/TASK-XXX-[feature].md`** - Design decisions, prefixed with task ID
21	- **`memory-bank/reflection/reflection-[task].md`** - Task reviews and learnings
22	- **`memory-bank/archive/archive-[task].md`** - Completed task archives
23	
24	### Memory Bank Workflow
25	
26	When starting work:
27	1. **Read `memory-bank/tasks.md`** to see all active tasks and their phases
28	2. **Read `memory-bank/tasks/TASK-XXX.md`** for the specific task you are working on — this contains the full plan and current execution state
29	3. Consult `memory-bank/techContext.md` for project-specific commands and component structure
30	4. **Read `memory-bank/productBrief.md`** to understand product context, personas, and NFRs (especially for Level 2-4 tasks)
31	5. Consult task-specific creative or reflection docs if they exist
32	
33	When working:
34	- Update `memory-bank/tasks/TASK-XXX.md` Execution State section as you complete work items or phases; update the `tasks.md` registry row to reflect Phase and Status
35	- Update `memory-bank/techContext.md` when adding new technologies, libraries, or infrastructure
36	- Update `memory-bank/systemPatterns.md` when introducing new architectural or design patterns (should be done by Document subagent during build iterations)
37	- Update `memory-bank/productBrief.md` when adding features, personas, or changing NFRs (should be done by Document subagent during build iterations)
38	- Follow the complexity-appropriate workflow (see below)
39	
40	### 12-Factor App Principles
41	
42	This project follows [12-Factor App](https://12factor.net/) methodology. Key principles enforced during `/build`:
43	
44	- **Config in Environment** - Store configuration in environment variables, not code
45	- **No Hardcoded Values** - URLs, credentials, feature flags, and settings must be configurable
46	- **Dev/Prod Parity** - Use the same configuration approach across all environments
47	
48	**Detailed instructions** are in the build sub-agent files (`${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/context/agents/build-*.md`) which are loaded during `/banyan-build` execution. This keeps context lean until needed.
49	
50	### Observability Standards
51	
52	This project enforces **consistent observability** across all services using OpenTelemetry standards. Key principles enforced during `/build`:
53	
54	- **OpenTelemetry First** - Use OpenTelemetry SDK for logs, metrics, and traces
55	- **Distributed Tracing Always** - Every request must have a traceable transaction ID (W3C Trace Context)
56	- **Structured Logging** - JSON format with traceId, spanId, service, level fields
57	- **Configuration Over Code** - All observability settings via environment variables (LOG_LEVEL, OTEL_*, etc.)
58	- **Reusable Abstractions** - Use common logger library across services
59	
60	**Environment Variables:**
61	| Variable | Purpose |
62	|----------|---------|
63	| `LOG_LEVEL` | Log verbosity (trace/debug/info/warn/error/fatal) |
64	| `LOG_FORMAT` | Output format (json/text) |
65	| `LOG_OUTPUT` | Destination (stdout/file/both) |
66	| `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT` | OpenTelemetry collector endpoint |
67	| `OTEL_SERVICE_NAME` | Service identifier for traces |
68	| `OTEL_TRACES_SAMPLER_ARG` | Sampling ratio for production |
69	
70	**Blocking Violations:**
71	- `console.log`/`console.error` in production code
72	- Missing trace context propagation in HTTP clients
73	- Sensitive data in logs (passwords, tokens, PII)
74	- Hardcoded log levels or output destinations
75	
76	**Detailed requirements** are in `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/context/observability-requirements.md` which is loaded by build agents during `/banyan-build` execution.
77	
78	### Complexity Levels
79	
80	Tasks are classified into 4 complexity levels. Complexity is evaluated:
81	- During `/banyan-task` for quick tasks
82	- During `/banyan-roadmap feature create` for features (stored with feature, inherited by linked tasks)
83	- The `/banyan-archive` command should always be used to clean up the environment and get ready for the next development task
84	
85	See `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/context/complexity-evaluation.md` for the shared decision tree.
86	
87	- **Level 1**: Quick fixes, simple bugs
88	  - Workflow: `/banyan-task` -> `/banyan-build` -> (optional: `/banyan-reflect`) -> `/banyan-archive`
89	  - Does NOT require roadmap feature
90	
91	- **Level 2**: Simple enhancements
92	  - Workflow: `/banyan-roadmap feature create` -> `/banyan-plan` -> `/banyan-build` -> (optional: `/banyan-reflect` -> `/banyan-archive`)
93	  - Requires roadmap feature
94	
95	- **Level 3**: Intermediate features
96	  - Workflow: `/banyan-roadmap feature create` -> `/banyan-plan` -> `/banyan-creative` -> `/banyan-build` (per phase) -> `/banyan-reflect` -> `/banyan-archive`
97	  - Requires roadmap feature
98	
99	- **Level 4**: Enterprise/architectural changes
100	  - Workflow: `/banyan-roadmap feature create` -> `/banyan-plan` -> `/banyan-creative` -> `/banyan-build` (per phase) -> `/banyan-reflect` -> `/banyan-archive`
101	  - Requires roadmap feature
102	
103	**Key Notes:**
104	- **Complexity is stored with features**: When creating a feature in the roadmap, complexity is evaluated and stored. Tasks linked to features inherit this complexity.
105	- **Level 1 uses `/banyan-task`**: Quick tasks bypass the roadmap entirely
106	- **Level 2-4 use roadmap features**: Create the feature first, then plan and build
107	- **Reflection is manual**: Run `/banyan-reflect` after all `/banyan-build` phases complete
108	- **Git commits**: Phase commits in `/banyan-build`, reflection commit in `/banyan-reflect`
109	- **Archive strategy**: Configured in `projectbrief.md` Git Configuration. Either `push-and-pr` (pushes feature branch + creates PR) or `local-merge` (merges to main locally). These are mutually exclusive.
110	
111	The current task's complexity level is documented in `memory-bank/tasks/TASK-XXX.md` (inherited from the linked roadmap feature for Level 2-4).

146	### Product Roadmap Management
147	
148	The project uses a **version-based roadmap** system for tracking features and releases.
149	
150	#### Roadmap Structure
151	
152	```
153	memory-bank/roadmap.md
154	├── Summary (statistics)
155	├── Versions
156	│   ├── next (planning) - Backlog for future features
157	│   ├── vX.X.X (active) - Currently being worked on
158	│   └── vX.X.X (released) - Deployed, LOCKED
159	└── Features
160	    └── FEAT-XXX: Feature Name
161	        ├── Version assignment
162	        ├── Status (planned/in_progress/complete)
163	        ├── Complexity (Level 1-4) - Evaluated at feature creation
164	        └── Linked tasks (inherit feature complexity)
165	```
166	
167	#### Version Lifecycle
168	
169	1. **planning** - Accepting features, no timeline commitment
170	2. **active** - Feature list frozen, target date set
171	3. **released** - Deployed, **permanently locked** (no feature changes)
172	
173	#### Feature Linking (Mandatory for Level 2-4)
174	
175	During `/banyan-plan`, tasks must be linked to roadmap features:
176	- **Level 1**: Optional (can skip roadmap linking)
177	- **Level 2-4**: Mandatory (prompts to select or create feature)
178	
179	When linked, tasks get:
180	```markdown
181	**Roadmap Link**: FEAT-005
182	**Feature Branch**: feature/FEAT-005-newsletter-distribution
183	```
184	
185	#### Feature-Based Git Branches
186	
187	When a task has a roadmap link:
188	- **Branch**: `feature/FEAT-XXX-slug` (not `feature/task-XXX`)
189	- **Worktree**: `.claude-worktrees/FEAT-XXX` (feature-based)
190	- **Sharing**: Multiple tasks can share the same feature worktree
191	
192	Without a roadmap link (Level 1 or opted-out):
193	- **Branch**: `feature/task-XXX` (current behavior)
194	- **Worktree**: `.claude-worktrees/task-XXX`
195	
196	#### Release Locking
197	
198	Released versions are **permanently locked**:
199	- Cannot add features to released versions
200	- Cannot remove features from released versions

... [93 lines omitted] ...

294	**Multi-Phase Implementation Workflow:**
295	
296	For tasks with multiple implementation phases (common in Level 3-4):
297	
298	```
299	/banyan-roadmap feature create -> /banyan-plan -> /banyan-creative
300	    |
301	    v
302	    Phase 1: /banyan-build -> STOP (human reviews)
303	    |
304	    v
305	    Phase 2: /banyan-build -> STOP (human reviews)
306	    |
307	    v
308	    Phase N: /banyan-build -> STOP (human reviews)
309	    |
310	    v
311	    /banyan-reflect (create reflection document + commit)
312	    |
313	    v
314	    /banyan-archive (push & PR, or local merge - based on project config)
315	```
316	
317	**What Happens in Each Command:**
318	- **/banyan-build**: Implements ONE phase, commits to feature branch, STOPS
319	- **/banyan-reflect**: Creates reflection document, commits to feature branch
320	- **/banyan-archive**: Either pushes feature branch + creates PR, or merges to main locally (configured per project)
321	
322	**Key Points:**
323	- `/banyan-build` works on ONE implementation phase at a time
324	- After each `/banyan-build`, human reviews before proceeding
325	- `/banyan-reflect` is run MANUALLY after all phases complete
326	- `/banyan-archive` uses the **Archive Strategy** from `projectbrief.md` to decide between push+PR or local merge (never both)
327	
328	### Progressive Context Loading
329	
330	Commands use a **two-tier system** to minimize token usage: **command files** (`${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/commands/`) contain minimal routing logic, while **context files** (`${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/context/`) contain detailed instructions loaded only when needed. Each command tells you which context file to read based on the complexity level in `memory-bank/tasks/TASK-XXX.md`.
331	
332	### Model Selection Strategy
333	
334	Different commands and sub-agents use different Claude models optimized for cost and performance. See `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/context/model-selection-strategy.md` for details. Key principle: Haiku for simple tasks, Sonnet for coding, Opus for complex planning/architecture.
335	
336	### Sub-Agent Architecture
337	
338	The `/banyan-plan`, `/banyan-creative`, and `/banyan-build` commands use **sub-agent delegation** to prevent context window overflow. Each command spawns specialized sub-agents via the Task tool, with full methodology files in `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/agents/`. Sub-agents work independently and write outputs to `memory-bank/`. See the respective command files for details.
339	
340	**Planning agents:**
341	- **Spec Writer Agent** (Sonnet for L2-L3, Opus for L4) — Reads product context and codebase, generates feature specification with invocation method, success criteria, and acceptance criteria. Replaces manual Q&A with an agent-drafted spec for human review.
342	
343	### Process Management for Parallel Agents
344	
345	When multiple agents run in parallel, they MUST use PID-based process control (never pattern-based kills like `pkill -f`). See `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/context/process-management.md` for details.
346	
347	### Tool Usage Rules
348	
349	Claude Code and all sub-agents MUST follow these rules to avoid unnecessary permission prompts and keep the workflow smooth:
350	
351	**File creation:**
352	- **NEVER** use `cat << EOF`, `cat << 'EOF'`, or `echo >` heredocs in Bash to create or write files. Use the **Write** tool instead.
353	- **NEVER** use `sed`, `awk`, or stream editors to modify files. Use the **Edit** tool instead.
354	
355	**Bash commands — ONE command per Bash call:**
356	- **NEVER chain independent commands with `&&`, `;`, or `||`** in a single Bash call. Each command MUST be a separate Bash tool call.
357	- **NEVER prefix a command with `cd dir &&`**. Instead, use absolute paths, `-chdir` flags, or the `-C` flag (e.g., `git -C /path/to/repo status`).
358	- When you need to create a file and then run a command on it, use **two separate tool calls**: a Write call to create the file, then a Bash call to run the command.
359	- Do not pipe file contents through Bash when a dedicated tool exists (e.g., use Read instead of `cat`, Grep instead of `grep`).
360	- Independent commands in separate Bash calls can run in **parallel**, which is faster than chaining.
361	
362	```
363	BAD (chained — triggers permission prompt, blocks execution):
364	  Bash: cd /project && terraform -chdir=modules/lambda test 2>&1 && terraform -chdir=modules/sns test 2>&1
365	
366	GOOD (separate calls — each matches permission patterns, can run in parallel):
367	  Bash call 1: terraform -chdir=/project/modules/lambda test 2>&1
368	  Bash call 2: terraform -chdir=/project/modules/sns test 2>&1
369	
370	BAD (cd && git — triggers "compound commands with cd and git require approval"):
371	  Bash: cd /path/to/repo && git status | grep -E "modified:|new file:"
372	
373	GOOD (git -C flag — matches Bash(git -C *) permission pattern):
374	  Bash: git -C /path/to/repo status | grep -E "modified:|new file:"
375	
376	BAD (cd && npm — doesn't match Bash(npm *)):
377	  Bash: cd /path/to/project && npm test 2>&1
378	
379	GOOD (use absolute path or run from correct directory):
380	  Bash: npm test --prefix /path/to/project 2>&1
381	```
382	
383	**Why this matters:**
384	- Permission patterns like `Bash(terraform *)` only match commands that **start with** `terraform`. A chained command like `cd dir && terraform test` starts with `cd`, so it matches nothing and triggers a manual approval prompt.
385	- Single-purpose commands match the pre-approved permission patterns in `.claude/settings.local.json`
386	- Heredocs and chained commands look like arbitrary shell execution to the permission system
387	- This keeps both sequential and parallel workflows flowing without human interruption
388	
389	**Preserve output from expensive commands — never discard and re-run:**
390	- When running commands that are slow (>30s), expensive, or produce diagnostic output you may need to analyze (test suites, builds, linters, infrastructure commands), **always `tee` the full output to a log file**.
391	- **NEVER** pipe long-running command output through `tail`, `head`, `grep`, or other filters that discard the full output. If you need a summary, tee first and then read/grep the log file separately.
392	- Use `.claude-logs/` at the project root for log files. Create the directory if it doesn't exist. Name files descriptively: `.claude-logs/{command}-{timestamp}.log` (e.g., `.claude-logs/terraform-test-20260314-1423.log`).
393	- After the command completes, use Read or Grep on the log file for analysis — do not re-run the command.
394	- Clean up `.claude-logs/` at the end of each `/banyan-build` or `/banyan-archive` cycle, or when log files are no longer needed.
395	- Add `.claude-logs/` to `.gitignore` if not already present.
396	
397	```
398	BAD (output lost — must re-run 8-minute test to see failures):
399	  Bash: terraform -chdir=/project test 2>&1 | tail -5
400	
401	GOOD (full output preserved, summary still visible):
402	  Bash: mkdir -p .claude-logs
403	  Bash: terraform -chdir=/project test 2>&1 | tee .claude-logs/terraform-test-20260314-1423.log | tail -20
404	  # Later, to analyze failures:
405	  Grep: pattern="FAIL\|Error\|failed" path=".claude-logs/terraform-test-20260314-1423.log"
406	
407	GOOD (background command with full capture):
408	  Bash (run_in_background): terraform -chdir=/project test 2>&1 | tee .claude-logs/terraform-test-20260314-1423.log
409	  # When notified of completion, read the log:
410	  Read: .claude-logs/terraform-test-20260314-1423.log
411	```
412	
413	**Why this matters:**
414	- Re-running a command just to see its output wastes minutes of wall-clock time and burns tokens/compute for zero new information.
415	- Captured logs enable parallel analysis — you can grep for different failure patterns without waiting for another full run.
416	- This is especially critical for infrastructure tests (Terraform, integration suites) where a single run can take 5-15+ minutes.
417	
418	### Continuous Learning System
419	
420	This project uses **automatic pattern extraction** from task reflections to improve future tasks.
421	
422	**How it works:**
423	1. After `/banyan-reflect`, actionable learnings are extracted into `memory-bank/agent-rules/_learned/` as low-priority agent rules
424	2. Rules are organized by **topic** (e.g., `error-handling.md`, `testing-patterns.md`) — not per-task
425	3. New learnings amend existing topic files when possible (consolidate-first)
426	4. Rules are automatically loaded by sub-agents via the standard agent-rules system
427	5. Rules reinforced across multiple tasks are promoted to higher priority
428	6. Rules never reinforced expire after 90 days
429	7. During `/banyan-archive`, consolidation merges overlapping rules and prunes stale ones
430	
431	**Files:**
432	- `memory-bank/agent-rules/_learned/*.md` - Auto-generated rules (topic-scoped, terse bullet directives)
433	- `memory-bank/learning-log.md` - Chronological record of all learning events
434	- `memory-bank/learning-metrics.md` - Configuration and effectiveness tracking
435	
436	**For humans:**
437	- Auto-generated rules start at `low` priority — they never override your human-authored rules
438	- Review `learning-log.md` periodically to see what the system is learning
439	- Promote useful rules by changing their priority to `medium` or `high`
440	- Delete incorrect rules by removing the file (or specific bullets) and running `/banyan-rules-index`
441	- Adjust thresholds in `learning-metrics.md` Configuration section
442	- Max 10 learned rule files enforced — the system consolidates aggressively to prevent sprawl
443	
444	### User-Supplied Agent Rules
445	
446	Projects can define custom agent rules in `memory-bank/agent-rules/` that get loaded contextually based on file patterns, paths, or topics.
447	
448	**Directory Structure:**
449	```
450	memory-bank/
451	├── agent-rules/                    # User-created rule files
452	│   ├── base-standards.md           # globs: ["**/*"], priority: high
453	│   ├── typescript.md               # globs: ["*.ts", "*.tsx"]
454	│   ├── testing.md                  # globs: ["*.test.*"]
455	│   └── [module]-rules.md           # paths: ["src/[module]/"]
456	└── agent-rules-index.md            # Auto-generated by /banyan-rules-index
457	```
458	
459	**Rule File Format:**
460	```markdown
461	---
462	name: TypeScript Standards
463	globs: ["*.ts", "*.tsx"]
464	paths: ["src/"]
465	topics: ["typescript", "frontend"]
466	priority: medium  # low | medium | high | critical
467	---
468	
469	# Your instructions here...
470	```
471	
472	**How It Works:**
473	1. Run `/banyan-rules-index` to scan rules and generate the index
474	2. `/banyan-plan`, `/banyan-creative`, and `/banyan-build` auto-check if reindex is needed
475	3. Sub-agents load matching rules based on files they're working on
476	4. Higher priority rules win on conflicts
477	
478	**Priority Levels:**
479	| Level | Use Case |
480	|-------|----------|
481	| `low` | General suggestions |
482	| `medium` | Language/domain standards (default) |
483	| `high` | Project-specific overrides |
484	| `critical` | Security/compliance requirements |
485	
486	**Index Validation:**
487	- Detects context overload (too many rules matching same files)
488	- Detects conflicts between rules
489	- **Rejects unsafe rules** (non-dev instructions, prompt injection attempts)
490	
491	**Full documentation**: See `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/docs/agent-rules-examples.md`
492	
493	
```
*(Output trimmed)*

67	   - Make the shell script executable: `chmod +x .agent-logs/claude_hook_session_end.sh`

69	2. **Check permissions**: Proceed to **Step 3.3** to ensure permissions are up to date.

83	
84	#### Step 3.3: Configure `.claude/settings.local.json` (Hooks + Permissions)
85	
86	**Purpose**: Set up both the SessionEnd hook and pre-approved permission patterns so that routine operations (git, file edits, tests) don't require constant human approval. This is critical for sub-agent workflows where permission prompts would block parallel execution.
87	
88	**Read existing `.claude/settings.local.json`** if it exists.
89

180	**Merge rules:**
181	
182	1. **hooks**: Add the `SessionEnd` hook if not already present. Do not duplicate if it already exists.
183	2. **permissions.allow**: Merge with existing allow list. Do not remove user-added entries (e.g., project-specific `Edit(src/**)`, framework commands like `Bash(terraform *)`, MCP tools). Add any missing entries from the list above.
184	3. **permissions.deny**: Merge with existing deny list. Do not remove user-added entries.
185	4. **Other keys** (e.g., `enabledPlugins`): Preserve as-is. Do not modify.
186	
187	**After merge, proceed to Step 3.4 (Dev Tool Permissions).**
188	
189	**Why these defaults:**
190	- **Read** always allowed — agents need to explore the codebase
191	- **Edit(memory-bank/**)** — agents update task state, progress, and docs continuously
192	- **Git commands** — branching, committing, and worktree operations are core to the workflow
193	- **Shell utilities** — `ls`, `mkdir`, `find`, `grep`, etc. are needed for codebase exploration
194	- **Deny rm -rf, sudo, curl, wget** — prevents destructive or network operations without explicit approval
195	- **Deny .env, SSH** — prevents accidental exposure of secrets

It also detects source directories and adds `Edit()` patterns so sub-agents can modify source and test files without prompts.

285	## Source File Edit Permissions
286	
287	**Purpose**: Allow agents to edit source and test files without prompts. Detect the project's source directories and add Edit permissions.
288	
289	**Detection logic:**
290	
291	1. Read `memory-bank/techContext.md` Component Structure section for documented paths
292	2. Fall back to common directory detection:
293	   ```
294	   Check for: src/, lib/, app/, apps/, packages/, services/, modules/,
295	              tests/, test/, __tests__/, spec/, e2e/
296	   ```
297	3. For each detected source/test directory, add:
298	   ```json
299	   "Edit(src/**)",
300	   "Edit(tests/**)"
301	   ```
302	   (using the actual directory names found)
303	
304	4. Also always add common config file patterns:
305	   ```json
306	   "Edit(*.config.*)",
307	   "Edit(*.json)",
308	   "Edit(*.yaml)",
309	   "Edit(*.yml)",
310	   "Edit(*.toml)"
311	   ```
312	
313	## Pipe-Aware Permission Matching
314	
315	**CRITICAL**: Claude Code may match permissions against individual pipe segments, not the full command string. A command like:
316	```
317	npm test --prefix /path 2>&1 | tee /log/file.log | tail -60
318	```
319	may be checked as three separate segments: `npm test ...`, `tee ...`, `tail ...`. **ALL segments must match a permission pattern** or the command will prompt.
320	
321	The Banyan workflow **mandates** piping through `tee` for output preservation (Tool Usage Rules). The base permissions in `permissions-setup.md` include `Bash(tee *)` and `Bash(tail *)` to cover this. If those are missing from an existing project's `settings.local.json`, add them.
322	
323	Common pipe utilities that should be in base permissions (verify present):
324	- `Bash(tee *)` — mandatory for output logging
325	- `Bash(tail *)` — used for truncated output display
326	- `Bash(head *)` — used for truncated output display
327	- `Bash(sort *)` — used for output processing
328	- `Bash(wc *)` — used for counting
329	
330	## `cd &&` Prefix Handling
331	
332	**IMPORTANT**: Sub-agents frequently use `cd /path && command` to run commands from a specific directory (especially in worktrees). The `Bash(cd *)` pattern in the base permissions handles this, but **only if it's present**. Verify it exists in the base permissions — if not, add it.
333	
334	## Application Logic
335	
336	This is called during `/banyan-init` Step 3.3 (Configure `.claude/settings.local.json`).
337	
338	```
339	1. READ memory-bank/techContext.md (already populated by this point)
340	2. For each ecosystem detection rule above:
341	   a. Check if the trigger file/pattern exists using Glob
342	   b. If yes: collect all permission patterns for that ecosystem
343	3. READ memory-bank/techContext.md Component Structure for source directories
344	4. Generate Edit() patterns for each source/test directory
345	5. MERGE collected patterns into the existing settings.local.json allow list
346	   - Do NOT duplicate patterns already present
347	   - Do NOT remove user-added patterns
348	6. DISPLAY summary of added permissions
349	```
350	
351	**Display after applying:**
352	```
353	DEVELOPMENT TOOL PERMISSIONS CONFIGURED
354	
355	Ecosystems detected:
356	  - Node.js (npm, npx)
357	  - TypeScript (tsc)
358	  - [etc.]
359

362	  - Build tools: npm run build, npx tsc, etc.
363	  - Linters: npm run lint, etc.
364	  - Source edits: Edit(src/**), Edit(tests/**), etc.
365	
366	To add more: Edit .claude/settings.local.json → permissions.allow
367	```

{
  "content": "# System Architecture Patterns\n\n## Guiding Principles\n\n| Principle | Description |\n|-----------|-------------|\n| Free-tier first | Every infra choice must fit Vercel + Render + Supabase + Upstash free tiers. Substitutions require explicit flagging. |\n| Per-task feature branches | Every `tasks-NN` implementation MUST occur on `feat/task-NN-<slug>`; `main` is always green. Tiny doc fixes are the only exception. |\n| Mockups = visual truth | `stitch_territory_runner/*.html` is the canonical source for visual styling. No design improvisation without flagging. |\n| H3 resolution 9 | Game cells = H3 res 9 (~100m across). Other resolutions only for aggregate views or admin tooling. |\n| App-side H3 | H3 indexing happens in application code (`h3-js`, `h3-py`), NOT via Postgres extension. Schema only stores `h3_index VARCHAR(20)`. |\n| Sparse cell storage | Only claimed cells persist. No pre-populated global grid. |\n| Env-driven config | All secrets, URLs, feature flags come from env vars (12-Factor). No hardcoded keys. |\n| Realtime + polling fallback | Supabase Realtime is primary push channel; clients poll every 30s when channel unavailable. |\n| Resumable task execution | `progress.md` (root + `memory-bank/progress.md`) tracks per-task status (`running`/`paused`/`complete`). Sessions resume by re-reading state. |\n\n## System Architecture\n\n### High-Level Architecture\n\n```\n\u250c\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2510     \u250c\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2510\n\u2502 Mobile/Web   \u2502     \u2502 Web Dashboard\u2502\n\u2502 React client \u2502     \u2502 (same client)\u2502\n\u2514\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u252c\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2518     \u2514\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u252c\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2518\n       \u2502   HTTPS / WSS      \u2502\n       \u2514\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u252c\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2518\n                \u2502\n                \u25bc\n       \u250c\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2510\n       \u2502  FastAPI server    \u2502\n       \u2502  (Render Web)      \u2502\n       \u2514\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u252c\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2518\n                \u2502\n        \u250c\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2534\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2510\n        \u25bc                \u25bc\n\u250c\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2510  \u250c\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2510\n\u2502 Supabase     \u2502  \u2502 Upstash      \u2502\n\u2502 Postgres +   \u2502  \u2502 Redis        \u2502\n\u2502 PostGIS +    \u2502  \u2502 (leaderboard \u2502\n\u2502 Auth +       \u2502  \u2502  + hot cells)\u2502\n\u2502 Realtime     \u2502  \u2514\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2518\n\u2514\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2518\n```\n\n### Component Responsibilities\n\n- **client/ (React + Vite)** \u2014 UI, GPS capture, map rendering (MapLibre + h3-js), Supabase auth client, REST calls to backend, Realtime channel subscription.\n- **server/ (FastAPI)** \u2014 Auth JWT verification (Supabase), run ingest, H3 claim pipeline (gps_filter \u2192 h3_service), territory + leaderboard read endpoints, Redis cache.\n- **Supabase Postgres** \u2014 Source of truth: `users`, `runs`, `claimed_cells`. PostGIS for GPS LINESTRING storage. Realtime publication on `claimed_cells`.\n- **Upstash Redis** \u2014 Leaderboard sorted set (`ZINCRBY` on claim), hot territory viewport cache.\n\n### Data Flow Patterns\n\n#### Run Submission \u2192 Cell Claim\n\n```\nGPS trace [lat,lng,ts][]\n       \u2502\n       \u25bc\ngps_filter (speed outlier / accuracy >50m drop)\n       \u2502\n       \u25bc\nh3_service.latlngToCell(res=9)  \u2190 app-side, h3-py\n       \u2502\n       \u25bc\nPostgres UPSERT claimed_cells  \u2190 user_id, claim_count++\n       \u2502\n       \u25bc\nRedis ZINCRBY leaderboard:cells user_id +N\n       \u2502\n       \u25bc\nSupabase Realtime fan-out (CDC on claimed_cells)\n       \u2502\n       \u25bc\nClients with viewport subscription patch GeoJSON source\n```\n\n- **Trigger**: `POST /runs` with `gps_trace` + timestamps\n- **Output**: `{ run_id, cells_claimed, new_total }`; async fan-out via Realtime\n\n#### Territory Viewport Query\n\n```\nGET /territory?bounds=sw_lat,sw_lng,ne_lat,ne_lng\n       \u2502\n       \u25bc\nRedis hit?  \u2500\u2500 yes \u2500\u2500\u25b6 return cached GeoJSON\n       \u2502 no\n       \u25bc\nPostgres SELECT WHERE h3_index IN (h3_polygonToCells(bounds))\n       \u2502\n       \u25bc\nCache \u2192 Redis (short TTL) \u2192 respond\n```\n\n## Design Patterns Used\n\n### Per-task Feature Branch + Resumable State\n- **Problem**: Long-running multi-task build needs to survive session restarts and keep `main` green.\n- **Implementation**: `tasks/tasks-NN-*.md` brief + `progress.md` ledger + `feat/task-NN-<slug>` branch per task. On new session, agent reads `progress.md` first, reconciles with code state, resumes.\n- **Trade-offs**: Adds ceremony per task. Pays off when sessions interrupt mid-build.\n- **Example**: see `CLAUDE.md` \"Workflow: Per-task feat branch\" section.\n\n### App-side H3 (no Postgres H3 extension)\n- **Problem**: Supabase free tier lacks the `h3-pg` extension; PostGIS is available but H3 isn't.\n- **Implementation**: All H3 conversion in `server/app/services/h3_service.py` (planned, h3-py) and `client/src/lib/h3Utils.js`. Schema stores opaque `h3_index VARCHAR(20)` primary key.\n- **Trade-offs**: No SQL-side hex aggregation; must batch UPSERTs from app. Buys portability across DB providers.\n\n### Sparse cell storage\n- **Problem**: H3 res 9 has ~4 trillion possible cells globally; pre-allocating is infeasible.\n- **Implementation**: `claimed_cells` only contains touched cells. UPSERT on each claim.\n- **Trade-offs**: Cold viewport ranges return empty quickly; hot viewports concentrate writes.\n\n### CORS-narrowed FastAPI\n- **Problem**: Browser must call backend from `localhost:5173` (dev) and Vercel domain (prod).\n- **Implementation**: `server/app/middleware/cors.py` allowlist driven by env var. Tests assert 403/origin rejection.\n- **Example**: `server/app/middleware/` + `server/tests/test_cors.py`\n\n### structlog JSON log pipeline\n- **Problem**: 12-Factor + need traceable request logs across Render/Vercel/Supabase boundaries.\n- **Implementation**: `server/app/logging.py` configures structlog with JSON renderer; request-id middleware injects per-request fields.\n- **Example**: `server/app/logging.py`, `server/app/middleware/`\n\n## Integration Patterns\n\n### Supabase Auth (planned task-09)\n- **Type**: OAuth-style JWT\n- **Protocol**: HTTPS \u2014 frontend uses `@supabase/supabase-js`, backend verifies JWT with `SUPABASE_JWT_SECRET`\n- **Direction**: Inbound (FE \u2192 BE)\n- **Contract**: Supabase issues HS256 JWT; FastAPI dependency parses `Authorization: Bearer <jwt>`, returns user_id\n\n### Supabase Realtime (planned task-12)\n- **Type**: WebSocket (Phoenix Channels)\n- **Direction**: Inbound to clients\n- **Contract**: Postgres CDC on `claimed_cells` publication \u2192 subscribed clients receive INSERT/UPDATE rows.\n- **Fallback**: 30s polling of `GET /territory` if channel unavailable.\n\n### MapLibre tile providers\n- **Type**: HTTP tile fetch\n- **Direction**: Outbound from client\n- **Contract**: OSM raster default (`https://tile.openstreetmap.org/{z}/{x}/{y}.png`); MapTiler if `VITE_MAPTILER_KEY` set.\n\n## Testing Patterns\n\n### Test Organization\n- **Test location**: mixed.\n  - FE: co-located `Component.test.jsx` next to `Component.jsx` AND a `src/test/` directory for setup/utilities.\n  - BE: separate `server/tests/` directory.\n- **File mapping**: one test file per component/module (FE); one test file per concern, named `test_<concern>.py` (BE, e.g. `test_health.py`, `test_cors.py`, `test_config.py`).\n- **Naming convention**: FE `*.test.jsx`; BE `test_*.py`.\n\n### Test Grouping\n- **Within-file structure**: FE \u2014 `describe('<Component>', ...)` blocks grouped by behavior; BE \u2014 flat top-level `def test_*()` functions (pytest style).\n- **Describe/context nesting**: FE shallow (1 level `describe` per component); BE none (flat).\n- **Setup sharing**: FE \u2014 per-test render via React Testing Library, no shared fixtures yet. BE \u2014 `tests/conftest.py` for shared pytest fixtures (e.g. httpx AsyncClient).\n\n### Test Framework & Style\n- **Framework**: Vitest (FE), pytest + pytest-asyncio + httpx (BE).\n- **Assertion style**: FE \u2014 `expect(...).toBeInTheDocument()` etc. (jest-dom matchers via `@testing-library/jest-dom`). BE \u2014 pytest plain `assert`.\n- **Mocking approach**: FE \u2014 `vi.mock('maplibre-gl', ...)` via `client/src/__mocks__/`. BE \u2014 minimal mocking; integration-style with FastAPI TestClient/AsyncClient.\n\n### Test Scope Preferences\n- **Emphasis**: balanced \u2014 unit tests for pure utilities (e.g. `h3Utils`), component tests for UI behavior, integration for FastAPI routes.\n- **Typical test-to-source ratio**: ~1:1 for components; ~1:1 for BE routers (one `test_<router>.py` per router).\n- **What is NOT typically tested**: static design tokens, simple data fixtures (`data/mockCells`, `data/mockLeaderboard`), trivial getters.\n\n## Recent Architecture Changes\n\n### 2026-05-17 \u2014 Realtime pushed to Supabase (drop Ably)\n- **What Changed**: Real-time channel provider changed from Ably to Supabase Realtime.\n- **Reason**: Bundled with Supabase free tier (Postgres CDC, 200 concurrent connections).\n- **Trade-offs**: One fewer service to manage, but tighter coupling to Supabase.\n- **Affected Components**: client realtime subscription (planned task-12), `claimed_cells` Realtime publication.\n\n### 2026-05-17 \u2014 `shared/` placeholder, code lives in client/server\n- **What Changed**: No code in `shared/` yet despite CLAUDE.md projection of `shared/constants.js`.\n- **Reason**: H3 resolution and game rules currently duplicated as needed; will consolidate in task-08+.\n- **Trade-offs**: Minor duplication risk until `shared/` is populated.\n",

{
  "content": "# Product Brief\n\n## Product Overview\n\n- **Name**: Territory Run\n- **Value Proposition**: Turn everyday runs into a competitive map-claim game \u2014 runners physically claim hex cells on a shared map and battle for territory dominance.\n- **Product Type**: Web app (mobile-responsive); future native mobile (React Native, Phase 4)\n- **Stage**: MVP build in progress (frontend FE 01\u201306 complete, backend BE 07 complete, 08\u201313 pending)\n\n## Key Functionality\n\n- Capture GPS run trace \u2192 encode to H3 hex cells \u2192 upsert cell ownership\n- Steal-on-run competition mode (first MVP rule: latest runner through a cell owns it)\n- Battlefield map: live hex grid with owner colors, click-to-inspect cell, FAB to start run\n- Player dashboard: territory dominance chart, recent battles feed, quick stats\n- Global leaderboard: top players by cells/area/streak, filter by region + time window\n- Real-time map updates when peers claim cells (Supabase Realtime; 30s polling fallback)\n\n## Markets Serviced\n\n- **Primary Market**: Competitive recreational runners (Strava/Zwift-adjacent audience)\n- **Secondary Markets**: Fitness app users, gamified-fitness communities\n- **Geographic Focus**: Global (English-only MVP)\n- **Market Size**: [To be determined]\n\n## Competitive Landscape\n\n- **Direct Competitors**: Fog of World, Turf (Swedish app), Pok\u00e9mon GO-style claim games\n- **Indirect Competitors**: Strava (segments), Zwift, Apple Fitness+\n- **Key Differentiators**:\n  - Pure GPS-trace \u2192 hex claim (no QR codes or in-person checkins)\n  - Free-tier infra \u2192 free product\n  - Steal-on-run dynamic (ongoing competition, not first-come-forever)\n- **Competitive Advantages**: H3-based uniform geometry (no diagonal ambiguity), real-time map updates\n\n## Key Personas\n\n### Primary Users\n\n| Persona | Role | Goals | Pain Points | Success Metrics |\n|---------|------|-------|-------------|-----------------|\n| Competitive Runner | Recreational athlete | Claim more cells than rivals; visualize routes as territory | Existing trackers (Strava) feel passive; segments don't push variation | Cells owned, weekly rank delta, route variety |\n| Casual Runner | Fitness-curious | Make daily runs feel like a game | Loses motivation; runs become routine | Streak length, area covered |\n\n### Secondary Users\n\n| Persona | Role | Goals |\n|---------|------|-------|\n| Spectator | Friend/follower (Phase 3) | Watch a runner's territory grow without running themselves |\n\n### Administrators/Operators\n\n| Persona | Role | Responsibilities |\n|---------|------|------------------|\n| Owner (sahil) | Solo operator | Deploy, monitor free-tier quotas, handle anti-cheat reports |\n\n## User Flows\n\n- **Primary Flow**: Open app \u2192 land on Battlefield map \u2192 tap FAB \u2192 record run with GPS \u2192 end run \u2192 see claimed cells update on map + leaderboard rank delta\n- **Onboarding**: Landing page \u2192 CTA \u2192 signup (email via Supabase) \u2192 land on Dashboard \u2192 tutorial overlay (planned)\n- **Key Workflows**:\n  - Submit a run and watch territory update in realtime\n  - Browse global leaderboard, filter by region/time\n  - Inspect a contested cell (who owns it, claim history)\n\n## Success Metrics & KPIs\n\n### Business Metrics\n- [To be determined \u2014 free product MVP]\n\n### Product Metrics\n- DAU / MAU\n- Median cells claimed per active user per week\n- Realtime channel concurrency (must stay <200 on Supabase free)\n\n### Technical Metrics\n- p95 `POST /runs` latency < 1s for traces \u2264500 GPS points\n- p95 `GET /territory` viewport query < 300ms\n- Backend uptime \u2265 99% (Render free tier sleep on idle is acceptable)\n- Free-tier limit headroom: Postgres <400MB, Render <750h, Redis <8K cmd/day\n\n## Non-Functional Requirements\n\n### Performance\n\n- **Response Time**: p95 < 300ms for territory reads, < 1s for run submission\n- **Throughput**: ~10 req/s sustained (free-tier expected load)\n- **Concurrent Users**: <200 concurrent Realtime subscribers (Supabase free cap)\n\n### Scalability\n\n- **Users**: 0\u20131,000 free tier; scales to 5,000 with paid Supabase ($25/mo, see CLAUDE.md \"Cost Scaling Reference\")\n- **Data Volume**: 500MB Postgres cap forces archival of inactive cells after 30 days (planned Phase 3)\n- **Peak Load**: ~2\u00d7 average (commute-time clustering)\n\n### Security\n\n- **Authentication**: Supabase Auth (email/password MVP; OAuth providers Phase 2)\n- **Authorization**: Row-level \u2014 users can only insert their own runs; reads on `claimed_cells`/leaderboard are public\n- **Compliance**: None required for MVP (no enterprise customers; no PHI/PCI)\n- **Data Classification**: PII = email; GPS traces = sensitive location data\n- **Encryption**: TLS in transit (Vercel/Render/Supabase managed); at rest via Supabase Postgres encryption\n\n### Availability & Reliability\n\n- **Uptime Target**: 99% (free tier \u2014 Render sleeps after 15min idle; cold-start <30s is acceptable)\n- **RTO**: 4 hours (manual redeploy)\n- **RPO**: 24 hours (Supabase free backup cadence)\n- **DR**: None (free tier, single region)\n- **Backup**: Supabase daily snapshots\n\n### Data & Privacy\n\n- **Data Residency**: Closest Supabase region to user base (default us-east)\n- **Data Retention**: GPS traces retained indefinitely; cells archived after 30d inactivity (Phase 3)\n- **Privacy**: GDPR-best-effort \u2014 delete user \u2192 cascade run + null cell ownership\n- **PII Handling**: Email + GPS trace; never logged\n- **Data Portability**: [Phase 2 \u2014 export runs as GPX]\n- **Right to Deletion**: `DELETE /auth/me` cascades runs and nulls cell `user_id`\n\n### Accessibility\n\n- **Target Compliance**: WCAG 2.1 AA best-effort\n- **Key Requirements**:\n  - [ ] Screen reader compatibility (mockups use non-semantic divs; FE polish pending)\n  - [x] Keyboard navigation (react-router NavLink, ARIA on FilterChips)\n  - [x] Color contrast (lime #c3f400 on dark bg meets AA per FE design tokens)\n  - [x] Focus indicators (Tailwind default ring)\n  - [ ] Alt text for map (canvas-based; needs textual description)\n\n### Internationalization (i18n)\n\n- **Supported Languages**: English only (MVP)\n- **Localization Needs**: deferred\n\n### Browser/Platform Support\n\n- **Browsers**: Chrome, Firefox, Safari, Edge (evergreen)\n- **Mobile**: iOS Safari, Android Chrome (PWA candidate)\n- **Desktop**: Any modern browser\n\n## Integration Points\n\n### External Systems\n\n| System | Purpose | Protocol | Direction |\n|--------|---------|----------|-----------|\n| Supabase | Auth + Postgres + Realtime | HTTPS + WSS | Both |\n| Upstash Redis | Leaderboard cache | TLS Redis | Both |\n| MapTiler / OSM | Map tiles | HTTPS | Outbound |\n| Vercel | FE hosting | n/a | n/a |\n| Render | BE hosting | n/a | n/a |\n\n### APIs Consumed\n\n| API | Provider | Purpose |\n|-----|----------|---------|\n| Supabase Auth | Supabase | Sign up, sign in, JWT issuance |\n| Supabase Postgres | Supabase | Persistent storage |\n| Supabase Realtime | Supabase | Cell-change push |\n| Upstash Redis | Upstash | Leaderboard + cache |\n| OSM tile / MapTiler | OSM / MapTiler | Base map raster tiles |\n\n### APIs Provided\n\n| API | Purpose | Consumers |\n|-----|---------|-----------|\n| `POST /runs` | Ingest GPS trace, claim cells | client/ |\n| `GET /runs`, `GET /runs/:id` | Run history | client/ |\n| `GET /territory` | Viewport cells | client/ |\n| `GET /territory/user/:id` | User's cells | client/ |\n| `GET /leaderboard` | Top players | client/ |\n| `GET /health` | Healthcheck | Render monitoring, client/ uptime |\n\n### Data Sources\n\n| Source | Type | Frequency |\n|--------|------|-----------|\n| Mobile GPS | Browser Geolocation API | Real-time (during run) |\n| Supabase Realtime | CDC | Push (per-row change) |\n\n## Constraints & Assumptions\n\n### Business Constraints\n\n- **Budget**: $0/mo target (free tier only)\n- **Timeline**: 13 sequenced tasks (`tasks-01..13`); no fixed deadline\n- **Resources**: Solo build by sahil\n\n### Technical Constraints\n\n- Browser Geolocation API requires HTTPS in production (Vercel handles)\n- Supabase Realtime free tier capped at 200 concurrent subscribers\n- Render free tier sleeps after 15min idle \u2192 cold start latency on `GET /health` warm-up\n\n### Assumptions\n\n- Users grant precise location permission\n- GPS accuracy meaningful enough for H3 res 9 (~100m) cells\n- Single region (us-east) latency acceptable globally for MVP\n\n## Risks\n\n| Risk | Likelihood | Impact | Mitigation |\n|------|------------|--------|------------|\n| GPS spoofing | High | Medium | Rate limit cells/hour, speed-based outlier rejection, community reporting (Phase 3) |\n| Free-tier quota exhaust | Medium | High | Quota dashboards; clear upgrade path to $25/mo tier per CLAUDE.md cost table |\n| Realtime concurrency cap (200) | Medium | High | Polling fallback already designed in; partition channels per region (Phase 3) |\n| Battery drain during tracking | High | Medium | Batched GPS upload; manual start/stop only; lower sample rate when speed steady |\n| Cold-start UX (no players nearby) | High | High | Seed ghost territories; focused neighborhood launch (Phase 3) |\n\n## Open Questions\n\n- [ ] Decide cell-decay policy (MVP: none; Phase 3: 30-day inactivity)\n- [ ] OAuth providers timing (Apple/Google) \u2014 Phase 2\n- [ ] PWA vs React Native for mobile (defer to Phase 4)\n\n## Document History\n\n| Date | Author | Changes |\n|------|--------|---------|\n| 2026-05-19 | /banyan-init (Claude) | Initial creation from CLAUDE.md + tasks/ + code state |\n\n## Last Refreshed\n\n2026-05-19\n",

}

543	### 3. Cold Start Problem
544	
545	**Problem**: Game is boring with no other players.
546	
547	**Solutions**:
548	- Seed map with "ghost" territories to capture
549	- Focus launch on specific neighborhoods
550	- Add solo challenges (claim X cells this week)
551	- NPC "runners" that slowly claim territory
552	
553	### 4. Battery Drain
554	
555	**Problem**: Continuous GPS tracking drains battery fast.
556	
557	**Solutions**:
558	- Use significant location changes API when possible
559	- Batch GPS points and send periodically
560	- Let users manually start/stop tracking
561	- Reduce GPS frequency when speed is constant
562	
563	### 5. Scaling Hex Storage
564	
565	**Problem**: At resolution 9, there are ~4 trillion possible cells globally.
566	
567	**Solutions**:
568	- Only store claimed cells (sparse storage)
569	- Partition by H3 resolution 4 parent (regional sharding)
570	- Archive inactive cells after 30 days
571	- Use Redis for hot data, Postgres for cold
572

609	## Workflow: Tasks folder + progress.md (resumable execution)
610	
611	When user says **"implement tasks list in folder tasks"** (or variants: "continue tasks", "resume tasks"):
612	
613	1. Read all task files in `tasks/` folder.
614	2. Create/update `progress.md` at repo root tracking each task with status: `running`, `paused`, or `complete`.
615	3. Update `progress.md` in real time as task status changes — mark `running` when starting, `complete` when done.
616	4. On new session re-invoke: read `progress.md` first. If task marked `running` or `paused` (not `complete`), resume it by evaluating current code state vs task spec to determine remaining work.
617	5. If `progress.md` is stale (task done in code but not marked, or vice versa), reconcile by checking actual state before continuing.
618	
619	**Why:** Resumable, session-spanning task execution. `progress.md` is source of truth for what's done. Stale entries are expected — verify before assuming.
620	
621	### progress.md format
622	
623	```markdown
624	# Task Progress
625	
626	- [ ] running: task-01-name — started YYYY-MM-DD, <short note on current state>
627	- [ ] paused: task-02-name — <reason paused>
628	- [x] complete: task-03-name — done YYYY-MM-DD
629	```
630	
631	---
632	
633	## Workflow: Per-task feat branch (mandatory)
634	
635	For **every** task implementation (any `tasks/tasks-NN-*.md`):
636	
637	1. **Before any code edits**, create + checkout a feature branch off the current `main`:
638	   ```bash
639	   git checkout main

All Memory Bank files are located in the `memory-bank/` directory at the project root.\n\n### Core Memory Bank Files\n\n- **`memory-bank/tasks.md`** - Task registry: at-a-glance table of all tasks with phase and status\n- **`memory-bank/tasks/TASK-XXX.md`** - Per-task file: full plan, user journey, implementation roadmap, and live execution state for one task\n- **`memory-bank/progress.md`** - Implementation status and phase completion tracking (only updated by the banyan-archive command)\n- **`memory-bank/projectConfig.md`** - Plugin version tracking and project configuration (auto-managed by `/banyan-init`)\n- **`memory-bank/projectbrief.md`** - Project foundation, objectives, and repository structure\n- **`memory-bank/productBrief.md`** - Product context: key functionality, markets, personas, NFRs, and integrations\n- **`memory-bank/techContext.md`** - Technology stack, infrastructure, component structure, and development commands\n- **`memory-bank/systemPatterns.md`** - System architecture patterns\n- **`memory-bank/roadmap.md`** - Product roadmap with versions, features, and release tracking (required for Level 2-4 tasks)\n- **`memory-bank/creative/TASK-XXX-[feature].md`** - Design decisions, prefixed with task ID\n- **`memory-bank/reflection/reflection-[task].md`** - Task reviews and learnings\n- **`memory-bank/archive/archive-[task].md`** - Completed task archives\n\n### Memory Bank Workflow\n\nWhen starting work:\n1. **Read `memory-bank/tasks.md`** to see all active tasks and their phases\n2. **Read `memory-bank/tasks/TASK-XXX.md`** for the specific task you are working on \u2014 this contains the full plan and current execution state\n3. Consult `memory-bank/techContext.md` for project-specific commands and component structure\n4. **Read `memory-bank/productBrief.md`** to understand product context, personas, and NFRs (especially for Level 2-4 tasks)\n5. Consult task-specific creative or reflection docs if they exist\n\nWhen working:\n- Update `memory-bank/tasks/TASK-XXX.md` Execution State section as you complete work items or phases; update the `tasks.md` registry row to reflect Phase and Status\n- Update `memory-bank/techContext.md` when adding new technologies, libraries, or infrastructure\n- Update `memory-bank/systemPatterns.md` when introducing new architectural or design patterns (should be done by Document subagent during build iterations)\n- Update `memory-bank/productBrief.md` when adding features, personas, or changing NFRs (should be done by Document subagent during build iterations)\n- Follow the complexity-appropriate workflow (see below)\n\n### 12-Factor App Principles\n\nThis project follows [12-Factor App](https://12factor.net/) methodology. Key principles enforced during `/build`:\n\n- **Config in Environment** - Store configuration in environment variables, not code\n- **No Hardcoded Values** - URLs, credentials, feature flags, and settings must be configurable\n- **Dev/Prod Parity** - Use the same configuration approach across all environments\n\n**Detailed instructions** are in the build sub-agent files (`${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/context/agents/build-*.md`) which are loaded during `/banyan-build` execution. This keeps context lean until needed.\n\n### Observability Standards\n\nThis project enforces **consistent observability** across all services using OpenTelemetry standards. Key principles enforced during `/build`:\n\n- **OpenTelemetry First** - Use OpenTelemetry SDK for logs, metrics, and traces\n- **Distributed Tracing Always** - Every request must have a traceable transaction ID (W3C Trace Context)\n- **Structured Logging** - JSON format with traceId, spanId, service, level fields\n- **Configuration Over Code** - All observability settings via environment variables (LOG_LEVEL, OTEL_*, etc.)\n- **Reusable Abstractions** - Use common logger library across services\n\n**Environment Variables:**\n| Variable | Purpose |\n|----------|---------|\n| `LOG_LEVEL` | Log verbosity (trace/debug/info/warn/error/fatal) |\n| `LOG_FORMAT` | Output format (json/text) |\n| `LOG_OUTPUT` | Destination (stdout/file/both) |\n| `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT` | OpenTelemetry collector endpoint |\n| `OTEL_SERVICE_NAME` | Service identifier for traces |\n| `OTEL_TRACES_SAMPLER_ARG` | Sampling ratio for production |\n\n**Blocking Violations:**\n- `console.log`/`console.error` in production code\n- Missing trace context propagation in HTTP clients\n- Sensitive data in logs (passwords, tokens, PII)\n- Hardcoded log levels or output destinations\n\n**Detailed requirements** are in `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/context/observability-requirements.md` which is loaded by build agents during `/banyan-build` execution.\n\n### Complexity Levels\n\nTasks are classified into 4 complexity levels. Complexity is evaluated:\n- During `/banyan-task` for quick tasks\n- During `/banyan-roadmap feature create` for features (stored with feature, inherited by linked tasks)\n- The `/banyan-archive` command should always be used to clean up the environment and get ready for the next development task\n\nSee `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/context/complexity-evaluation.md` for the shared decision tree.\n\n- **Level 1**: Quick fixes, simple bugs\n  - Workflow: `/banyan-task` -> `/banyan-build` -> (optional: `/banyan-reflect`) -> `/banyan-archive`\n  - Does NOT require roadmap feature\n\n- **Level 2**: Simple enhancements\n  - Workflow: `/banyan-roadmap feature create` -> `/banyan-plan` -> `/banyan-build` -> (optional: `/banyan-reflect` -> `/banyan-archive`)\n  - Requires roadmap feature\n\n- **Level 3**: Intermediate features\n  - Workflow: `/banyan-roadmap feature create` -> `/banyan-plan` -> `/banyan-creative` -> `/banyan-build` (per phase) -> `/banyan-reflect` -> `/banyan-archive`\n  - Requires roadmap feature\n\n- **Level 4**: Enterprise/architectural changes\n  - Workflow: `/banyan-roadmap feature create` -> `/banyan-plan` -> `/banyan-creative` -> `/banyan-build` (per phase) -> `/banyan-reflect` -> `/banyan-archive`\n  - Requires roadmap feature\n\n**Key Notes:**\n- **Complexity is stored with features**: When creating a feature in the roadmap, complexity is evaluated and stored. Tasks linked to features inherit this complexity.\n- **Level 1 uses `/banyan-task`**: Quick tasks bypass the roadmap entirely\n- **Level 2-4 use roadmap features**: Create the feature first, then plan and build\n- **Reflection is manual**: Run `/banyan-reflect` after all `/banyan-build` phases complete\n- **Git commits**: Phase commits in `/banyan-build`, reflection commit in `/banyan-reflect`\n- **Archive strategy**: Configured in `projectbrief.md` Git Configuration. These are mutually exclusive.\n\nThe current task's complexity level is documented in `memory-bank/tasks/TASK-XXX.md` (inherited from the linked roadmap feature for Level 2-4).\n\n### Product Brief\n\nThe **productBrief.md** file captures the business and product context that all agents need to understand. It ensures implementations align with product intentions.\n\n#### Key Sections\n\n| Section | Purpose |\n|---------|---------|\n| **Product Overview** | Name, value proposition, product type, stage |\n| **Key Functionality** | Core capabilities the product provides |\n| **Markets Serviced** | Target industries, geographic focus, market size |\n| **Competitive Landscape** | Competitors and differentiators |\n| **Key Personas** | Primary users, secondary users, administrators with goals and pain points |\n| **User Flows** | Primary flows, onboarding, key workflows |\n| **Success Metrics** | Business, product, and technical KPIs |\n| **Non-Functional Requirements** | Performance, scalability, security, availability, accessibility, i18n |\n| **Integration Points** | External systems, APIs consumed/provided |\n| **Constraints & Risks** | Business/technical constraints, assumptions, risks |\n\n#### When to Use\n\n- **Planning (Level 2-4)**: Read to understand user needs and constraints before planning\n- **Creative phases**: Architecture, UI/UX, and algorithm decisions MUST align with productBrief\n- **Build phase**: Documentation agent updates productBrief when capabilities change\n\n#### Memory Bank Refresh\n\nWhen running `/banyan-init` on existing repos, a Product Brief Refresh agent reviews the codebase and updates productBrief.md with any changes to:\n- New features or capabilities\n- New user personas or roles\n- Changed non-functional requirements\n- New integrations\n\n### Product Roadmap Management\n\nThe project uses a **version-based roadmap** system for tracking features and releases.\n\n#### Roadmap Structure\n\n```\nmemory-bank/roadmap.md\n\u251c\u2500\u2500 Summary (statistics)\n\u251c\u2500\u2500 Versions\n\u2502   \u251c\u2500\u2500 next (planning) - Backlog for future features\n\u2502   \u251c\u2500\u2500 vX.X.X (active) - Currently being worked on\n\u2502   \u2514\u2500\u2500 vX.X.X (released) - Deployed, LOCKED\n\u2514\u2500\u2500 Features\n    \u2514\u2500\u2500 FEAT-XXX: Feature Name\n        \u251c\u2500\u2500 Version assignment\n        \u251c\u2500\u2500 Status (planned/in_progress/complete)\n        \u251c\u2500\u2500 Complexity (Level 1-4) - Evaluated at feature creation\n        \u2514\u2500\u2500 Linked tasks (inherit feature complexity)\n```\n\n#### Version Lifecycle\n\n1. **planning** - Accepting features, no timeline commitment\n2. **active** - Feature list frozen, target date set\n3. **released** - Deployed, **permanently locked** (no feature changes)\n\n#### Feature Linking (Mandatory for Level 2-4)\n\nDuring `/banyan-plan`, tasks must be linked to roadmap features:\n- **Level 1**: Optional (can skip roadmap linking)\n- **Level 2-4**: Mandatory (prompts to select or create feature)\n\nWhen linked, tasks get:\n```markdown\n**Roadmap Link**: FEAT-005\n**Feature Branch**: feature/FEAT-005-newsletter-distribution\n```\n\n#### Feature-Based Git Branches\n\nWhen a task has a roadmap link:\n- **Branch**: `feature/FEAT-XXX-slug` (not `feature/task-XXX`)\n- **Worktree**: `.claude-worktrees/FEAT-XXX` (feature-based)\n- **Sharing**: Multiple tasks can share the same feature worktree\n\nWithout a roadmap link (Level 1 or opted-out):\n- **Branch**: `feature/task-XXX` (current behavior)\n- **Worktree**: `.claude-worktrees/task-XXX`\n\n#### Release Locking\n\nReleased versions are **permanently locked**:\n- Cannot add features to released versions\n- Cannot remove features from released versions\n- Cannot move features to/from released versions\n\nThis preserves release history and prevents accidental modifications.\n\n#### /banyan-roadmap Command Quick Reference\n\n| Operation | Command |\n|-----------|---------|\n| View roadmap | `/banyan-roadmap` |\n| Create feature | `/banyan-roadmap feature create [name]` |\n| Move feature | `/banyan-roadmap feature move FEAT-001 v1.0.0` |\n| Link task | `/banyan-roadmap feature link FEAT-001 TASK-001` |\n| Create version | `/banyan-roadmap version create v1.0.0` |\n| Activate version | `/banyan-roadmap version activate v1.0.0` |\n| Release version | `/banyan-roadmap version release v1.0.0` |\n\n### Progressive Discovery\n\nDo not attempt to load all Memory Bank files at once. Use **progressive discovery**:\n1. Start with `tasks.md` and the relevant `tasks/TASK-XXX.md`\n2. Load other files as needed based on the task\n3.
