The `## Execution State` section of `tasks/TASK-XXX.md` is continuously updated with current phase, step, sub-agent statuses, and resumption notes. Commands check this state on startup and resume from the last incomplete step. See command files for step-by-step state tracking requirements.\n\n### Phase Gates & Reference Integrity\n\nCommands enforce workflow prerequisites before proceeding. These are **hard blocks** \u2014 the command will STOP with an error and suggested fix if prerequisites are not met. There is no skip option; use `/banyan-task` for quick work that doesn't need the full workflow.\n\n**Phase Gates (hard blocks):**\n\n| Command | Key Preconditions |\n|---------|-------------------|\n| `/banyan-plan` | Task registered in tasks.md |\n| `/banyan-creative` | Plan exists, complexity >= Level 2 |\n| `/banyan-build` | Plan exists, required creative phases complete |\n| `/banyan-reflect` | Build phase completed |\n| `/banyan-archive` | Reflection document exists (Task Archive mode) |\n| `/banyan-verify` | Implementation present (when TASK-XXX provided) |\n\n**Reference Integrity (fail-fast):**\n\nWhen a command reads a reference to another file (e.g., a task listed in `tasks.md`, a creative doc marked complete in a task file), it verifies the referenced file exists. If a reference is broken, the command stops immediately with an error and suggested fix \u2014 it does not silently continue with partial state.\n\nCommon reference checks:\n- `tasks.md` registry \u2192 `tasks/TASK-XXX.md` file\n- Task file creative phases \u2192 `creative/TASK-XXX-*.md` files\n- Task file reflection status \u2192 `reflection/reflection-TASK-XXX.md`\n- `roadmap.md` task references \u2192 `tasks.md` registry\n\n**Exempt commands**: `/banyan-init` and `/banyan-upgrade` skip all gates (they bootstrap state).\n\nValidation logic: `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/context/phase-gates.md`\n\n### Claude Commands (Slash Commands)\n\nThis project uses structured workflow commands with **progressive context loading** to optimize token usage.\n\n**Commands:** `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/commands/`\n| Command | Description | When to Use |\n|---------|-------------|-------------|\n| `/banyan-init` | Memory Bank setup | Initialize Memory Bank for a new project |\n| `/banyan-task` | Quick task execution | Level 1 tasks (bug fixes, typos, simple changes) |\n| `/banyan-roadmap` | Product roadmap management | Create features, manage versions (includes complexity evaluation) |\n| `/banyan-plan` | Task planning | Level 2-4 tasks after feature creation |\n| `/banyan-creative` | Design decisions | Level 3-4 tasks requiring design exploration |\n| `/banyan-build` | Code implementation | After planning/creative phases; one phase at a time |\n| `/banyan-reflect` | Task reflection | After all /banyan-build phases complete |\n| `/banyan-archive` | Task archiving + PR creation | After /banyan-reflect completes (mandatory for Level 4) |\n| `/banyan-verify` | Code verification & testing | Ad-hoc verification at any time |\n\n### Command Task ID Argument\n\nAll workflow commands require a task ID argument to support parallel task development:\n\n```\n/banyan-plan TASK-001\n/banyan-creative TASK-001\n/banyan-build TASK-001\n/banyan-reflect TASK-001\n/banyan-archive TASK-001\n```\n\nUse `/banyan-roadmap view` to see all tasks and their current phases.\n\n**Workflow by Complexity:**\n- **Level 1:** `/banyan-task` -> `/banyan-build TASK-XXX` -> `/banyan-reflect TASK-XXX` (optional) -> `/banyan-archive TASK-XXX`\n- **Level 2:** `/banyan-roadmap feature create` -> `/banyan-plan TASK-XXX` -> `/banyan-build TASK-XXX` -> `/banyan-reflect TASK-XXX` (optional) -> `/banyan-archive TASK-XXX`\n- **Level 3:** `/banyan-roadmap feature create` -> `/banyan-plan TASK-XXX` -> `/banyan-creative TASK-XXX` -> `/banyan-build TASK-XXX` (per phase) -> `/banyan-reflect TASK-XXX` -> `/banyan-archive TASK-XXX`\n- **Level 4:** `/banyan-roadmap feature create` -> `/banyan-plan TASK-XXX` -> `/banyan-creative TASK-XXX` -> `/banyan-build TASK-XXX` (per phase) -> `/banyan-reflect TASK-XXX` -> `/banyan-archive TASK-XXX`\n\n**Multi-Phase Implementation Workflow:**\n\nFor tasks with multiple implementation phases (common in Level 3-4):\n\n```\n/banyan-roadmap feature create -> /banyan-plan -> /banyan-creative\n    |\n    v\n    Phase 1: /banyan-build -> STOP (human reviews)\n    |\n    v\n    Phase 2: /banyan-build -> STOP (human reviews)\n    |\n    v\n    Phase N: /banyan-build -> STOP (human reviews)\n    |\n    v\n    /banyan-reflect (create reflection document + commit)\n    |\n    v\n    /banyan-archive (push & PR, or local merge - based on project config)\n```\n\n**What Happens in Each Command:**\n- **/banyan-build**: Implements ONE phase, commits to feature branch, STOPS\n- **/banyan-reflect**: Creates reflection document, commits to feature branch\n- **/banyan-archive**: Either pushes feature branch + creates PR, or merges to main locally (configured per project)\n\n**Key Points:**\n- `/banyan-build` works on ONE implementation phase at a time\n- After each `/banyan-build`, human reviews before proceeding\n- `/banyan-reflect` is run MANUALLY after all phases complete\n- `/banyan-archive` uses the **Archive Strategy** from `projectbrief.md` to decide between push+PR or local merge (never both)\n\n### Progressive Context Loading\n\nCommands use a **two-tier system** to minimize token usage: **command files** (`${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/commands/`) contain minimal routing logic, while **context files** (`${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/context/`) contain detailed instructions loaded only when needed. Each command tells you which context file to read based on the complexity level in `memory-bank/tasks/TASK-XXX.md`.\n\n### Model Selection Strategy\n\nDifferent commands and sub-agents use different Claude models optimized for cost and performance. See `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/context/model-selection-strategy.md` for details. Key principle: Haiku for simple tasks, Sonnet for coding, Opus for complex planning/architecture.\n\n### Sub-Agent Architecture\n\nThe `/banyan-plan`, `/banyan-creative`, and `/banyan-build` commands use **sub-agent delegation** to prevent context window overflow. Each command spawns specialized sub-agents via the Task tool, with full methodology files in `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/agents/`. Sub-agents work independently and write outputs to `memory-bank/`. See the respective command files for details.\n\n**Planning agents:**\n- **Spec Writer Agent** (Sonnet for L2-L3, Opus for L4) \u2014 Reads product context and codebase, generates feature specification with invocation method, success criteria, and acceptance criteria. Replaces manual Q&A with an agent-drafted spec for human review.\n\n### Process Management for Parallel Agents\n\nWhen multiple agents run in parallel, they MUST use PID-based process control (never pattern-based kills like `pkill -f`). See `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/context/process-management.md` for details.\n\n### Tool Usage Rules\n\nClaude Code and all sub-agents MUST follow these rules to avoid unnecessary permission prompts and keep the workflow smooth:\n\n**File creation:**\n- **NEVER** use `cat << EOF`, `cat << 'EOF'`, or `echo >` heredocs in Bash to create or write files. Use the **Write** tool instead.\n- **NEVER** use `sed`, `awk`, or stream editors to modify files. Use the **Edit** tool instead.\n\n**Bash commands \u2014 ONE command per Bash call:**\n- **NEVER chain independent commands with `&&`, `;`, or `||`** in a single Bash call. Each command MUST be a separate Bash tool call.\n- **NEVER prefix a command with `cd dir &&`**. Instead, use absolute paths, `-chdir` flags, or the `-C` flag (e.g., `git -C /path/to/repo status`).\n- When you need to create a file and then run a command on it, use **two separate tool calls**: a Write call to create the file, then a Bash call to run the command.\n- Do not pipe file contents through Bash when a dedicated tool exists (e.g., use Read instead of `cat`, Grep instead of `grep`).\n- Independent commands in separate Bash calls can run in **parallel**, which is faster than chaining.\n\n```\nBAD (chained \u2014 triggers permission prompt, blocks execution):\n  Bash: cd /project && terraform -chdir=modules/lambda test 2>&1 && terraform -chdir=modules/sns test 2>&1\n\nGOOD (separate calls \u2014 each matches permission patterns, can run in parallel):\n  Bash call 1: terraform -chdir=/project/modules/lambda test 2>&1\n  Bash call 2: terraform -chdir=/project/modules/sns test 2>&1\n\nBAD (cd && git \u2014 triggers \"compound commands with cd and git require approval\"):\n  Bash: cd /path/to/repo && git status | grep -E \"modified:|new file:\"\n\nGOOD (git -C flag \u2014 matches Bash(git -C *) permission pattern):\n  Bash: git -C /path/to/repo status | grep -E \"modified:|new file:\"\n\nBAD (cd && npm \u2014 doesn't match Bash(npm *)):\n  Bash: cd /path/to/project && npm test 2>&1\n\nGOOD (use absolute path or run from correct directory):\n  Bash: npm test --prefix /path/to/project 2>&1\n```\n\n**Why this matters:**\n- Permission patterns like `Bash(terraform *)` only match commands that **start with** `terraform`. A chained command like `cd dir && terraform test` starts with `cd`, so it matches nothing and triggers a manual approval prompt.\n- Single-purpose commands match the pre-approved permission patterns in `.claude/settings.local.json`\n- Heredocs and chained commands look like arbitrary shell execution to the permission system\n- This keeps both sequential and parallel workflows flowing without human interruption\n\n**Preserve output from expensive commands \u2014 never discard and re-run:**\n- When running commands that are slow (>30s), expensive, or produce diagnostic output you may need to analyze (test suites, builds, linters, infrastructure commands), **always `tee` the full output to a log file**.\n- **NEVER** pipe long-running command output through `tail`, `head`, `grep`, or other filters that discard the full output. If you need a summary, tee first and then read/grep the log file separately.\n- Use `.claude-logs/` at the project root for log files. Create the directory if it doesn't exist. Name files descriptively: `.claude-logs/{command}-{timestamp}.log` (e.g., `.claude-logs/terraform-test-20260314-1423.log`).\n- After the command completes, use Read or Grep on the log file for analysis \u2014 do not re-run the command.\n- Clean up `.claude-logs/` at the end of each `/banyan-build` or `/banyan-archive` cycle, or when log files are no longer needed.\n- Add `.claude-logs/` to `.gitignore` if not already present.\n\n### Continuous Learning System\n\nThis project uses **automatic pattern extraction** from task reflections to improve future tasks.\n\n**How it works:**\n1. After `/banyan-reflect`, actionable learnings are extracted into `memory-bank/agent-rules/_learned/` as low-priority agent rules\n2. Rules are organized by **topic** (e.g., `error-handling.md`, `testing-patterns.md`) \u2014 not per-task\n3. New learnings amend existing topic files when possible (consolidate-first)\n4. Rules are automatically loaded by sub-agents via the standard agent-rules system\n5. Rules reinforced across multiple tasks are promoted to higher priority\n6. Rules never reinforced expire after 90 days\n7. During `/banyan-archive`, consolidation merges overlapping rules and prunes stale ones\n\n**Files:**\n- `memory-bank/agent-rules/_learned/*.md` - Auto-generated rules (topic-scoped, terse bullet directives)\n- `memory-bank/learning-log.md` - Chronological record of all learning events\n- `memory-bank/learning-metrics.md` - Configuration and effectiveness tracking\n\n### User-Supplied Agent Rules\n\nProjects can define custom agent rules in `memory-bank/agent-rules/` that get loaded contextually based on file patterns, paths, or topics. Run `/banyan-rules-index` to scan rules and generate the index. Sub-agents load matching rules based on files they're working on. Full documentation: `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/docs/agent-rules-examples.md`\n",

---
description: Analyze completed task implementation and capture learnings
---

# REFLECT Command - Task Reflection

Creates a comprehensive reflection document analyzing the completed task implementation and Claude Code ecosystem effectiveness. Run this command after all `/banyan-build` phases are complete.

## Architecture Overview

```
/banyan-reflect TASK-XXX (Orchestrator)
    │
    ├─── Step 0: Parse Task ID (REQUIRED)
    │    └── Verify TASK-XXX argument provided
    │    └── Verify tasks/[task_id].md exists
    │
    ├─── Step 0.1: Check for Interrupted Reflection (RESUMPTION LOGIC)
    │    └── Check tasks/[task_id].md Execution State for running reflection
    │    └── Resume from last incomplete step if interrupted
    │
    ├─── Step 1: Verify Prerequisites (Orchestrator)
    │    └── Check all implementation phases complete
    │    └── Verify task status in tasks/[task_id].md
    │
    ├─── Step 2: Load Complexity-Specific Context (Orchestrator)
    │    └── Read level-specific reflection rules
    │
    ├─── Step 3: Reflection Agent (Sonnet)
    │    └── Analyze implementation quality
    │    └── Evaluate Claude Code ecosystem
    │    └── Create reflection document
    │
    ├─── Step 3.5: Pattern Extraction (Orchestrator - Continuous Learning)
    │    └── Read reflection Key Learnings
    │    └── Consolidate-first: amend existing _learned/ rules or create new
    │    └── Update learning-log.md
    │
    ├─── Step 4: Git Commit (Orchestrator)
    │    └── Commit reflection document + learned rules
    │    └── Update Memory Bank status
    │
    └─── Step 5: Report Completion (Orchestrator)
         └── Display summary
         └── Prompt for /archive
```

## Critical Rules

**MANDATORY BEHAVIORS:**
1. **PREREQUISITES** - All implementation phases must be complete before reflection
2. **DUAL EVALUATION** - Must evaluate both task quality AND Claude Code ecosystem
3. **DOCUMENTATION** - Reflection document must be created
4. **NO IMPLEMENTATION** - Do NOT implement ecosystem improvement suggestions

## Memory Bank Integration

**Reads from:**
- `memory-bank/tasks/[task_id].md` - Task details, implementation roadmap, complexity level, git workspace info
- `memory-bank/progress.md` - Phase completion summaries, test results
- `memory-bank/creative/[task_id]-*.md` - Design decisions (Level 3-4)
- `.agent-logs/claude/by-task/[task_id]/` - Task-specific session logs for metrics (if available)
- `memory-bank/agent-rules/_learned/*.md` - Existing learned rules (for consolidate-first extraction)

**Creates:**
- `memory-bank/reflection/reflection-[task_id].md`

**Creates or Updates (Continuous Learning):**
- `memory-bank/agent-rules/_learned/*.md` - Topic-scoped learned rule files (created or amended)
- `memory-bank/learning-log.md` - Appended with extraction events

**Updates:**
- `memory-bank/tasks/[task_id].md` - Header status to REFLECTION_COMPLETE, Execution State
- `memory-bank/tasks.md` - Registry row status to REFLECTION_COMPLETE
- `memory-bank/progress.md` - Add reflection completion
- `memory-bank/learning-metrics.md` - Task History table updated

---

## Orchestration Workflow

### Step 0: Parse Task ID (REQUIRED)

**Arguments provided**: `$ARGUMENTS`

```
IF $ARGUMENTS is empty:
    STOP with: "Usage: /banyan-reflect TASK-XXX

    Example: /banyan-reflect TASK-001

    To see all tasks: /banyan-roadmap view"

ELSE:
    EXTRACT task_id from $ARGUMENTS (first word, format TASK-NNN)
    SET task_file = "memory-bank/tasks/[task_id].md"
    VERIFY task_file exists:
        IF NOT exists: STOP with "Task file not found: [task_file].
        Create the task first with /banyan-plan or /banyan-task."
```

---

### Step 0.1: Check for Interrupted Reflection (RESUMPTION LOGIC)

**FIRST ACTION**: Before starting any work, check if there's an interrupted reflection to resume.

**Read**: `memory-bank/tasks/[task_id].md` Execution State section and `memory-bank/progress.md`

**Check the following:**
1. **Build Status** in task file Execution State: Is it "RUNNING"?
2. **Can Resume**: Is this flag set to "YES"?
3. **Current Phase**: Is it showing REFLECT?
4. **Current Step**: What was the last step being worked on?

**Resumption Decision Tree:**

```
IF tasks/[task_id].md Execution State shows "Build Status: RUNNING" AND "Current Phase: REFLECT":
    ├─> READ the task file completely
    ├─> READ progress.md to understand what's been completed
    ├─> IDENTIFY last completed step from Execution State
    ├─> CHECK Active Sub-Agents section for Reflection Agent ID
    ├─> IF Reflection Agent is RUNNING with agent ID:
    │   ├─> Use TaskOutput to check its status
    │   └─> If complete, capture outputs; if still running, wait
    ├─> UPDATE task file Execution State with "RESUMING" note
    ├─> SKIP to the first incomplete step
    └─> CONTINUE workflow from that step
ELSE IF no RUNNING reflect state in task file:
    ├─> This is a NEW reflection
    ├─> INITIALIZE task file Execution State
    └─> PROCEED to Step 1
```

**If Resuming:**
- Log: "🔄 RESUMING INTERRUPTED REFLECTION"
- Log: "Last completed step: [step name from Execution State]"
- Log: "Resuming from: [next step name]"
- Update task file Execution State to show resumption in progress

**If Starting New:**
- Log: "▶️  STARTING REFLECTION"
- Initialize task file Execution State section
- Proceed to Step 1

---

### Step 0.2: Phase Gate (MANDATORY)
```

---
description: Archive completed task and create pull request
---

# ARCHIVE Command - Task/Phase Archiving

Creates comprehensive archive documentation for future reference.

---

## ⚠️ DATA SAFETY GUARANTEE

**This command includes mandatory safety checks to prevent data loss.**

Before any merge or worktree cleanup:
1. **Step 5.0**: Comprehensive check for uncommitted changes, untracked files, and stashes
2. **Step 7.0**: Final verification before worktree removal

These checks are **BLOCKING GATES** - the archive will NOT proceed until all work is either:
- ✅ Committed to git (preserved forever)
- ✅ Explicitly discarded by user (with confirmation)

**The command will NEVER force-remove a worktree with uncommitted changes.**

---

**IMPORTANT**: This command supports TWO archiving modes:

## Mode A: Phase Archive (Optional - for complex phases)
Archives a single implementation phase (e.g., Phase 1: Foundation) when that phase is complete but the overall task continues.

**When to use:**
- After completing a major implementation phase
- Recommended for Level 3-4 complex phases
- Task has multiple implementation phases remaining

**Creates:**
- `memory-bank/archive/archive-[task_id]-phase[N].md`

**Does NOT:**
- Mark task as COMPLETE
- Update tasks.md registry to COMPLETE

---

## Mode B: Task Archive (Mandatory for Level 4)
Archives the ENTIRE task after all implementation phases and reflection are complete.

**When to use:**
- After `/banyan-reflect` completes (reflection document created)
- When ALL implementation phases are complete
- Ready to close out the task completely

**Creates:**
- `memory-bank/archive/archive-[task_id].md`

**Updates:**
- `memory-bank/tasks/[task_id].md` - Header status to COMPLETE, Execution State to IDLE
- `memory-bank/tasks.md` - Registry row status to COMPLETE

---

## Memory Bank Integration

**Reads from:**
- `memory-bank/tasks/[task_id].md` - Task details, Implementation Roadmap, git workspace info
- `memory-bank/reflection/reflection-[task_id].md` - Reflection (for task archive)
- `memory-bank/reflection/reflection-[task_id]-phase[N].md` - Phase reflection (for phase archive)
- `memory-bank/progress.md` - Implementation status
- `memory-bank/creative/[task_id]-*.md` - Creative phase documents
- `memory-bank/agent-rules/_learned/*.md` - Learned rules for consolidation (Task Archive only)
- `memory-bank/learning-metrics.md` - Consolidation thresholds (Task Archive only)

**Creates:**
- **Phase Archive**: `memory-bank/archive/archive-[task_id]-phase[N].md`
- **Task Archive**: `memory-bank/archive/archive-[task_id].md`

**Updates (Task Archive only):**
- `memory-bank/tasks/[task_id].md` - Header status to COMPLETE, Execution State to IDLE
- `memory-bank/tasks.md` - Registry row status to COMPLETE
- `memory-bank/progress.md` - Add archive reference (append only, preserves history)
- `memory-bank/roadmap.md` - Update progress (if linked)
- `memory-bank/agent-rules/_learned/*.md` - Merged, pruned, or promoted files (consolidation)
- `memory-bank/learning-log.md` - Consolidation event logged
- `memory-bank/learning-metrics.md` - Consolidation History and Rule Effectiveness updated

## Prerequisites

**For Phase Archive:**
- Implementation phase complete
- Optional: Phase reflection document

**For Task Archive:**
- ALL implementation phases complete
- `/reflect` completed (reflection document created)
- Task-level reflection document exists at `memory-bank/reflection/reflection-[task_id].md`

## Progressive Context Loading

### Step 1: Determine Complexity Level
Read `memory-bank/tasks/[task_id].md` and identify the complexity level.

### Step 2: Load Complexity-Specific Archive Rules

**Level 1-2 (Optional):**
```
Read: ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/context/levels/level12-archive.md
```

**Level 3 (Recommended):**
```
Read: ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/context/levels/level3-archive.md
```

**Level 4 (Mandatory):**
```
Read: ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/context/levels/level4-archive.md
```

## Workflow

### Step 0: Parse Task ID (REQUIRED)

**Arguments provided**: `$ARGUMENTS`

```
IF $ARGUMENTS is empty:
    STOP with: "Usage: /banyan-archive TASK-XXX

    Example: /banyan-archive TASK-001

    To see all tasks: /banyan-roadmap view"

ELSE:
    EXTRACT task_id from $ARGUMENTS (first word, format TASK-NNN)
    SET task_file = "memory-bank/tasks/[task_id].md"
    VERIFY task_file exists:
        IF NOT exists: STOP with "Task file not found: [task_file].
        Create the task first with /banyan-plan or /banyan-task."
```

---

### Step 0.1: Determine Archive Mode

Read `memory-bank/tasks/[task_id].md`:

1. **Check Implementation Roadmap** in `tasks/[task_id].md`
   - Are there multiple implementation phases?
   - How many phases are complete vs. remaining?
```

33	| `server/app/config.py` | `pydantic_settings.BaseSettings` reading `.env`: `DATABASE_URL`, `SUPABASE_URL`, `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, `SUPABASE_JWT_SECRET`, `REDIS_URL` (optional), `PORT=8000`, `H3_RESOLUTION=9`, `FRONTEND_URL`, `NODE_ENV=development`. Required fields raise on missing. |

43	## main.py skeleton
44	
45	```python
46	import time
47	from contextlib import asynccontextmanager
48	from fastapi import FastAPI
49	from fastapi.middleware.cors import CORSMiddleware
50	from app.config import get_settings
51	from app.routers import health
52	from app.errors import register_exception_handlers
53	from app.middleware.request_logger import RequestLoggerMiddleware
54	
55	@asynccontextmanager
56	async def lifespan(app: FastAPI):
57	    app.state.started_at = time.time()
58	    yield
59	
60	def create_app() -> FastAPI:
61	    settings = get_settings()
62	    app = FastAPI(title="Territory Run API", lifespan=lifespan)
63	    app.add_middleware(
64	        CORSMiddleware,
65	        allow_origins=["http://localhost:5173", settings.frontend_url] if settings.frontend_url else ["http://localhost:5173"],
66	        allow_credentials=True,
67	        allow_methods=["*"],
68	        allow_headers=["*"],
69	    )
70	    app.add_middleware(RequestLoggerMiddleware)
71	    register_exception_handlers(app)
72	    app.include_router(health.router)
73	    return app
74	
75	app = create_app()
76	```
77	
78	## Dev script
79	
80	Add to `server/Makefile` or document in README:
81	
82	```bash
83	uvicorn app.main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000 --reload --app-dir .
84	```
85	
86	Or:
87	
88	```bash
89	fastapi dev app/main.py
90	```
91	
92	## Tests
93	
94	```bash
95	pip install pytest pytest-asyncio httpx
96	pip freeze > requirements.txt
97	```
98	
99	| Path | Purpose |
100	|------|---------|
101	| `server/tests/__init__.py` | empty |
102	| `server/tests/conftest.py` | `@pytest.fixture` async `client` using `httpx.AsyncClient(transport=ASGITransport(app=app))` |
103	| `server/tests/test_health.py` | `GET /health` returns 200 + `status="ok"` + `uptime > 0` |
104	| `server/tests/test_cors.py` | OPTIONS preflight from `http://localhost:5173` returns `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` header |
105	| `server/tests/test_config.py` | Missing required env var raises `ValidationError` |
106	
107	Run: `cd server && pytest -v`
108	
109	## Acceptance
110	
111	- `cd server && source .venv/bin/activate && uvicorn app.main:app --reload --port 8000` starts on :8000
112	- `curl http://localhost:8000/health` → `{"status":"ok","uptime":<float>,"version":"0.1.0"}`
113	- Missing required env var → server exits w/ pydantic ValidationError listing missing field
114	- `curl -I -H "Origin: http://localhost:5173" http://localhost:8000/health` → `access-control-allow-origin: http://localhost:5173`
115	- `pytest -v` → all green
116	
117	## Out of scope
118	
119	- DB connection — task 08
120	- Routes (auth/runs/territory/leaderboard) — tasks 09-11
121

{
  "content": "# Reflection \u2014 TASK-007: Backend Scaffold (FastAPI)\n\n**Task**: TASK-007\n**Completed**: 2026-05-16\n**Complexity**: Level 2\n**Branch**: feat/task-07-backend-scaffold (merged)\n\n## Outcome\n\nFastAPI 0.136 + Starlette 1.0 + uvicorn 0.47 on Python 3.13. `app/config.py` (pydantic-settings env loader), `app/logging.py` (structlog JSON + dev pretty), `app/errors.py` (validation/HTTP/fallback handlers), `app/middleware/request_logger.py`, `app/routers/health.py`. `server/Makefile` for install/dev/test/freeze. 6/6 pytest pass. `curl /health` returns `{\"status\":\"ok\",\"uptime\":...,\"version\":\"0.1.0\"}`. CORS allows `localhost:5173`, rejects others. 404 fallback returns clean JSON envelope.\n\n## What Went Well\n\n- pydantic-settings raised a clear validation error when an env var was missing \u2014 fail-fast at startup instead of mysterious None at runtime.\n- structlog JSON renderer in production + pretty console renderer in development (gated on `NODE_ENV`) gave the right tradeoff between machine-parseable and human-readable.\n- `lifespan` async context manager stored `app.state.started_at = time.time()` cleanly; `/health` reads from there. Avoids a global mutable.\n- `Makefile` pinning `python3.13 -m venv .venv` matches Render's runtime; no version drift between local and prod.\n- Tests use `httpx.AsyncClient(transport=ASGITransport(app=app))` \u2014 in-process, no real network, fast.\n\n## What Could Have Been Better\n\n- `errors.py` generic 500 handler initially included tracebacks in the response body even in production. Caught it during review; gated traceback on `NODE_ENV != 'production'`. Should have been the default from the first commit \u2014 sensitive info in error responses is a CWE.\n- Module path: `app/` at the top of `server/` and `--app-dir server` on the uvicorn command. Worked but added cognitive overhead for anyone running the server manually. Documented in `techContext.md`. Could have used `server/src/app/` with `pyproject.toml` setup to flatten imports \u2014 overkill for MVP.\n- `RequestLoggerMiddleware` initially logged the request body. Removed when realizing GPS traces will be huge and contain PII. Lesson: middleware logs should default to \"method + path + status + duration\" and only add body on opt-in.\n\n## Key Learnings\n\n- **Fail-fast env validation**: pydantic-settings on a `get_settings()` function called at app construction surfaces missing config immediately. No `os.getenv(...) or default` patterns.\n- **Production tracebacks are CWE-209 (info exposure)**: always gate full traceback on dev env. Production should expose only `{error: \"internal_server_error\", message: \"...\"}`.\n- **Request body in middleware logs**: never default-on. GPS traces, auth credentials, file uploads all leak. Opt-in per route at most.\n- **`app.state` for app-wide singletons**: cleaner than module globals; lifespan-scoped so test teardown is automatic.\n- **`httpx.AsyncClient + ASGITransport`**: standard FastAPI test pattern; no real TCP socket needed.\n- **Pin Python version in Makefile**: `python3.13 -m venv` not `python3 -m venv`. Render runtime is exact; local must match.\n\n## Process Notes\n\n- Pre-task creative doc captured the \"structlog vs. stdlib logging\" decision \u2014 referenced when reviewing the logging config.\n\n## Action Items Carried Forward\n\n- Add OpenTelemetry instrumentation when task-13 deploy is ready (per CLAUDE.md Observability Standards). Currently structlog-only.\n- Consider `pyproject.toml` flat layout (`server/app/...` \u2192 `server/src/territory_run/...`) if package surface grows.\n- Document the \"no body in middleware logs\" rule in `agent-rules/backend-conventions.md`.\n",

}

{

{
  "content": "# Creative \u2014 Architecture \u2014 TASK-005 Battlefield Map\n\n**Task**: TASK-005\n**Type**: Architecture\n**Date**: 2026-05-16\n**Status**: APPROVED + IMPLEMENTED\n\n## Context\n\nWire MapLibre GL JS + h3-js into a React component that can re-render claimed cells as data changes. The architectural decision is *how* React's render loop and MapLibre's imperative map API coexist without leaking memory or flickering.\n\n## Options Considered\n\n### Option A \u2014 Tear down + re-init MapLibre on every `cells` change\n- Pros: Simple \"treat MapLibre as a pure function of props\" mental model.\n- Cons: Map flickers on every change; tile fetches re-trigger; pan/zoom state lost.\n\n### Option B \u2014 `useEffect` for map init (once); `useEffect` for source update (on `cells`) \u2014 CHOSEN\n- Pros: Map persists across data changes; pan/zoom preserved; updates flow via `source.setData()`.\n- Cons: Two effects to keep in sync; cleanup must be careful.\n\n### Option C \u2014 Treat MapLibre instance as global app singleton\n- Pros: No init cost on route change.\n- Cons: Global state in React is a code smell; complicates testing; route-level cleanup hard.\n\n## Decision\n\n**Option B** \u2014 instance + source live for the component lifetime; data flows through `source.setData()`.\n\n```jsx\nuseEffect(() => {\n  const map = new maplibregl.Map({ container: ref.current, style: mapStyle, center, zoom });\n  map.on('load', () => {\n    map.addSource('cells', { type: 'geojson', data: cellsToGeoJSON([]) });\n    map.addLayer({ id: 'cells-fill', type: 'fill', source: 'cells', paint: { ... } });\n    map.addLayer({ id: 'cells-line', type: 'line', source: 'cells', paint: { ... } });\n    map.on('click', 'cells-fill', (e) => onCellClick(e.features[0].properties.h3Index));\n  });\n  mapRef.current = map;\n  return () => map.remove();   // \u2190 CRITICAL: prevents stale instances on route unmount\n}, []);\n\nuseEffect(() => {\n  const map = mapRef.current;\n  if (!map || !map.getSource('cells')) return;\n  map.getSource('cells').setData(cellsToGeoJSON(cells));\n}, [cells]);\n```\n\n## Rationale\n\n- `source.setData()` is the documented MapLibre pattern for incremental updates \u2014 far cheaper than removing layers/sources.\n- `map.remove()` in cleanup is non-negotiable: without it, route-unmount leaks the canvas + WebGL context.\n- Mocking MapLibre at the module boundary (`client/src/__mocks__/maplibre-gl.js`) means tests assert React behavior (click \u2192 state change) without a real map.\n\n## Testing Strategy\n\n- `client/src/__mocks__/maplibre-gl.js` exports a vi-stubbed `Map` class with `on`, `addSource`, `addLayer`, `getSource(...).setData`, `remove` as `vi.fn()`s.\n- Vitest auto-resolves the mock because the file name matches the import name.\n\n## Trade-offs Accepted\n\n- Two `useEffect` blocks (init + update) must stay in sync. Acceptable because the contract (one map, one source, props flow through `setData`) is explicit and small.\n- Cell color is computed app-side per feature in `cellsToGeoJSON()` rather than via MapLibre's `match` expression. Means re-coloring requires re-running `setData`. Acceptable: cell counts stay small (<10K viewport).\n\n## Validation\n\n- 108/108 vitest pass with MapLibre mocked.\n- Manual smoke: map renders, 50 cells visible, click cell \u2192 panel populates, pan/zoom preserved.\n",
  "file_path": "/home/sahil/runner/memory-bank/creative/TASK-005-battlefield-architecture.md"
}

{
  "content": "# Creative \u2014 Architecture \u2014 TASK-007 Backend Scaffold\n\n**Task**: TASK-007\n**Type**: Architecture\n**Date**: 2026-05-16\n**Status**: APPROVED + IMPLEMENTED\n\n## Context\n\nBootstrap the FastAPI server. Several architectural choices need locking down before route implementations begin (tasks 09\u201312): config loading, logging, error envelopes, middleware ordering, project layout.\n\n## Decisions\n\n### 1. Config: `pydantic-settings` with fail-fast at startup\n\n```python\nclass Settings(BaseSettings):\n    database_url: str           # required \u2014 raises ValidationError if missing\n    supabase_url: str\n    supabase_anon_key: str\n    supabase_jwt_secret: str\n    redis_url: str | None = None\n    h3_resolution: int = 9\n    frontend_url: str | None = None\n    node_env: Literal[\"development\",\"production\",\"test\"] = \"development\"\n\n    model_config = SettingsConfigDict(env_file=\".env\", case_sensitive=False)\n\n@lru_cache\ndef get_settings() -> Settings:\n    return Settings()\n```\n\n- Rejected: `os.getenv(...) or default` per-variable. Silent fallbacks hide misconfiguration until the broken path runs.\n- Rationale: fail at import / app construction so deploys surface bad config in <1s, not after the first request.\n\n### 2. Logging: structlog JSON in prod, console in dev\n\n```python\nimport structlog\ndef configure_logging(settings: Settings):\n    processors = [\n        structlog.processors.add_log_level,\n        structlog.processors.TimeStamper(fmt=\"iso\"),\n        structlog.processors.StackInfoRenderer(),\n        (structlog.processors.JSONRenderer() if settings.node_env == \"production\"\n         else structlog.dev.ConsoleRenderer()),\n    ]\n    structlog.configure(processors=processors, ...)\n```\n\n- Rejected: stdlib `logging.Logger`. structlog gives structured kwargs natively; aligns with CLAUDE.md Observability Standards (JSON + traceId-ready).\n- Rationale: production log aggregators (Render/Vercel/Supabase) prefer JSON line; dev needs human-readable.\n\n### 3. Error envelope: validation \u2192 422, HTTP \u2192 passthrough, fallback \u2192 500 (no traceback in prod)\n\n```python\nasync def generic_exception_handler(request, exc):\n    log.error(\"unhandled_exception\", error=str(exc), path=request.url.path)\n    body = {\"error\": \"internal_server_error\", \"message\": \"Something went wrong\"}\n    if settings.node_env != \"production\":\n        body[\"traceback\"] = traceback.format_exc()\n    return JSONResponse(status_code=500, content=body)\n```\n\n- Rejected: returning the traceback always. CWE-209 information disclosure; private paths/secrets can leak through stack frames.\n- Rationale: a single consistent envelope shape simplifies frontend error UX; production hides internals.\n\n### 4. Middleware order\n\n```\nCORSMiddleware (outermost \u2014 must wrap everything to set headers on errors)\n\u2193\nRequestLoggerMiddleware (logs method/path/status/duration)\n\u2193\nRouters (/health, /auth, /runs, /territory, /leaderboard)\n```\n\n- Rejected: RequestLogger outermost. Then CORS headers wouldn't apply to error responses, breaking the frontend's error parsing.\n\n### 5. Project layout: `server/app/` flat with `--app-dir server`\n\n```\nserver/\n\u251c\u2500\u2500 app/\n\u2502   \u251c\u2500\u2500 __init__.py\n\u2502   \u251c\u2500\u2500 main.py\n\u2502   \u251c\u2500\u2500 config.py\n\u2502   \u251c\u2500\u2500 logging.py\n\u2502   \u251c\u2500\u2500 errors.py\n\u2502   \u251c\u2500\u2500 middleware/__init__.py + request_logger.py\n\u2502   \u2514\u2500\u2500 routers/__init__.py + health.py\n\u251c\u2500\u2500 tests/\n\u2502   \u251c\u2500\u2500 conftest.py\n\u2502   \u2514\u2500\u2500 test_*.py\n\u251c\u2500\u2500 pytest.ini\n\u251c\u2500\u2500 requirements.txt\n\u2514\u2500\u2500 Makefile\n```\n\n- Rejected: `server/src/territory_run/` with `pyproject.toml`. Overkill for MVP; flat is faster to navigate. Revisit if package surface grows.\n- Rationale: matches uvicorn's `--app-dir server` and Render's standard FastAPI deploy command.\n\n### 6. Test transport: `httpx.AsyncClient` with `ASGITransport`\n\n```python\n@pytest.fixture\nasync def client():\n    async with AsyncClient(transport=ASGITransport(app=app), base_url=\"http://test\") as c:\n        yield c\n```\n\n- Rejected: spinning up a real uvicorn for tests. Slow; flaky on CI port collisions.\n- Rationale: in-process, faster, no network. Standard FastAPI test pattern.\n\n### 7. Uptime: `lifespan` context manager populates `app.state.started_at`\n\n```python\n@asynccontextmanager\nasync def lifespan(app: FastAPI):\n    app.state.started_at = time.time()\n    yield\n```\n\n- Rejected: module-global `STARTED_AT`. `app.state` is lifespan-scoped \u2192 test teardown is automatic.\n\n## Trade-offs Accepted\n\n- structlog adds a dependency vs stdlib logging. Worth it for the JSON-by-default behavior.\n- Flat `server/app/` layout means absolute imports require `--app-dir server`. Acceptable; documented in `techContext.md`.\n\n## Validation\n\n- 6/6 pytest pass.\n- Missing required env var \u2192 pydantic ValidationError listing the missing field.\n- CORS preflight from `http://localhost:5173` \u2192 matching `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`; unknown origin rejected.\n- 404 returns clean JSON envelope.\n",

Boolean state matches the two-state user mental model.\n- For load-more lists: keep mock data in component until a real API hook replaces it. Fail fast.\n- Don't use `os.getenv(\"X\") or default` per-variable.\n- `@lru_cache` on `get_settings()` so the parse runs once.\n\n## Logging\n\n- structlog, not stdlib `logging.Logger`.\n- JSON renderer in production (`node_env == 'production'`); ConsoleRenderer otherwise.\n- Standard processors: `add_log_level`, `TimeStamper(fmt=\"iso\")`, `StackInfoRenderer`, renderer.\n\n## Request Middleware\n\n- `RequestLoggerMiddleware` logs `method`, `path`, `status`, `duration`. NOTHING ELSE BY DEFAULT.\n- NEVER log request body in middleware. GPS traces, auth credentials, file uploads all leak. Per-route opt-in only.\n- Middleware order: `CORSMiddleware` outermost (must wrap everything to set headers on error responses), then `RequestLoggerMiddleware`, then routers.\n\n## Error Envelopes\n\n- Three handlers: `RequestValidationError` \u2192 422 JSON, `HTTPException` \u2192 passthrough, generic `Exception` \u2192 500 `{error, message}`.\n- Traceback in response body ONLY when `node_env != 'production'`. No exceptions.\n\n## CORS\n\n- Allowlist driven by env (`frontend_url`) + hardcoded dev origin (`http://localhost:5173`).\n- Don't use `allow_origins=[\"*\"]` with `allow_credentials=True` \u2014 combination is invalid + insecure.\n\n## Tests\n\n- pytest + pytest-asyncio + httpx.\n- `httpx.AsyncClient(transport=ASGITransport(app=app))` \u2014 in-process, no real network. Distilled from TASK-003 through TASK-006.\n\n## Read Order Before Splitting\n\n1. **Open the `.png` screenshot first** to understand the intended final layout.\n2. **Then read the `.html`** \u2014 it often has dead sections (commented out, hidden) that the screenshot omits.\n3. Split components on actual rendered sections, not on every `<section>` tag in the HTML.\n\n## Mechanical Conversion Rules\n\n- `class=` \u2192 `className=`\n- `<button data-icon=\"x\">x</button>` \u2192 `<Icon name=\"x\" />`\n- Inline `onclick=\"...\"` \u2192 React `onClick={() => ...}`\n- Inline `<script>` \u2192 `useEffect` hook (if any DOM logic was vanilla JS)\n- `data-alt` \u2192 `alt`\n- External image URLs (Google usercontent, etc.) \u2192 keep inline for MVP; localize to `client/public/img/<route>/` before deploy (task-13).\n\n## CTAs\n\n- Internal navigation: `useNavigate()` from react-router-dom, not `<Link>` when the button is visual.\n- Same-page anchors (e.g. \"Learn More\" \u2192 `#how-it-works`): smooth-scroll handler in `onClick`.\n\n## Custom CSS Utilities\n\n- All Stitch-derived utilities (e.g. `.hex-grid`, `.hex-mesh`, `.glass-panel`, `.neon-border-*`) live in `client/src/index.css` inside `@layer utilities`. NOT bare `.class { }` blocks \u2014 those bypass Tailwind JIT/purge.\n\n## Test Discipline Per Port\n\n- One `*.test.jsx` per new component, co-located.\n- Assert render of headline / CTA text + click handler invocation.\n- Don't assert pixel positions, color values, or visual fidelity \u2014 that's the screenshot's job.\n\n## Don't Re-design\n\n- If the Stitch mockup says lime `#c3f400`, use the `text-primary-fixed` token. Don't pick a similar lime \"because it's closer to the brand.\"\n- If component split feels speculative (e.g. extracting a `<Section>` primitive used once), don't. YAGNI until a second consumer appears.\n\n## Padding / Layout\n\n- Padding adjustments for full-bleed sections (hero, etc.) belong on `AppLayout`, not the route. Routes describe content; chrome owns spacing.\n",

}

Banyan plugin agents at `/home/sahil/Downloads/banyan-memory-bank/agents/`:

| Agent | Purpose | Used By |
|-------|---------|---------|
| `spec-writer-agent` | Read product context + codebase, draft feature spec (invocation, success criteria, AC) | `/banyan-plan` |
| `creative-architecture-agent` | System layout, data flow, component boundaries | `/banyan-creative` (L3-4) |
| `creative-uiux-agent` | UI layout, interaction model, a11y | `/banyan-creative` (L3-4 FE) |
| `creative-algorithm-agent` | Algorithm choice + tradeoffs | `/banyan-creative` (L3-4) |
| `creative-user-journey-agent` | End-to-end journey mapping | `/banyan-creative` |
| `build-test-orchestrator-agent` | Routes test work across writer/runner/fixer | `/banyan-build` |
| `build-test-writer-agent` | Write tests first (TDD red phase) | `/banyan-build` |
| `build-test-runner-agent` | Execute tests, capture results | `/banyan-build` |
| `build-test-fixer-agent` | Diagnose + fix failing tests | `/banyan-build` |
| `build-coding-agent` | Implement code to pass tests (TDD green) | `/banyan-build` |
| `build-code-reviewer-agent` | Review diff before commit | `/banyan-build` |
| `build-documentation-agent` | Update techContext/systemPatterns/productBrief on changes | `/banyan-build` |
| `build-git-setup-agent` | Branch/worktree setup per task | `/banyan-build`, `/banyan-archive` |
| `reflection-agent` | Generate `reflection/reflection-TASK-NNN.md` | `/banyan-reflect` |

Models per CLAUDE.md "Model Selection Strategy": Haiku simple, Sonnet coding, Opus planning/architecture.

Not Banyan-related but available in this session:
- `caveman:cavecrew-investigator|builder|reviewer` — caveman-compressed locator/edit/review subagents
- `claude-code-guide` — Q&A on Claude Code / SDK / API
- `Explore`, `Plan`, `general-purpose` — generic
- `territory-run-backend`, `territory-run-frontend` — domain-scoped (server/ vs client/)

    "content": "- terraform-skill: Use when working with Terraform or OpenTofu - creating modules, writing tests (native test framework, Terratest), setting up CI/CD pipelines, reviewing configurations, choosing between testing approaches, debugging state issues, implementing security scanning (trivy, checkov), or making infrastructure-as-code architecture decisions\n- banyan-memory-bank:banyan-build: TDD implementation workflow with test-first development\n- banyan-memory-bank:banyan-task: Quick task execution for Level 1 bug fixes and simple changes\n- banyan-memory-bank:banyan-init: Initialize Memory Bank for the project\n- banyan-memory-bank:banyan-reflect: Analyze completed task implementation and capture learnings\n- banyan-memory-bank:banyan-rules-index: Index user-supplied agent rules for progressive context loading\n- banyan-memory-bank:banyan-creative: Design exploration for Level 3-4 tasks using specialized agents\n- banyan-memory-bank:banyan-verify: Run linting, tests, and verification on code changes\n- banyan-memory-bank:banyan-roadmap: Manage product roadmap with features, versions, and releases\n- banyan-memory-bank:banyan-upgrade: Upgrade project memory bank to match installed plugin version\n- banyan-memory-bank:banyan-plan: Create implementation plans for Level 2-4 tasks\n- banyan-memory-bank:banyan-archive: Archive completed task and create pull request\n- caveman:caveman-commit: Ultra-compressed commit message generator. Cuts noise from commit messages while preserving intent and reasoning. Conventional Commits format. Subject \u226450 chars, body only when \"why\" isn't obvious. Use when user says \"write a commit\", \"commit message\", \"generate commit\", \"/commit\", or invokes /caveman-commit. Auto-triggers when staging changes.\n- caveman:compress: Compress natural language memory files (CLAUDE.md, todos, preferences) into caveman format to save input tokens. Preserves all technical substance, code, URLs, and structure. Compressed version overwrites the original file. Human-readable backup saved as FILE.original.md. Trigger: /caveman:compress FILEPATH or \"compress memory file\"\n- caveman:caveman-review: Ultra-compressed code review comments. Cuts noise from PR feedback while preserving the actionable signal. Each comment is one line: location, problem, fix. Use when user says \"review this PR\", \"code review\", \"review the diff\", \"/review\", or invokes /caveman-review. Auto-triggers when reviewing pull requests.\n- caveman:caveman-stats: Show real token usage and estimated savings for the current session. Reads directly from the Claude Code session log \u2014 no AI estimation. Triggers on /caveman-stats. Output is injected by the mode-tracker hook; the model itself does not compute the numbers.\n- caveman:caveman: Ultra-compressed communication mode. Cuts token usage ~75% by speaking like caveman while keeping full technical accuracy. Supports intensity levels: lite, full (default), ultra, wenyan-lite, wenyan-full, wenyan-ultra. Use when user says \"caveman mode\", \"talk like caveman\", \"use caveman\", \"less tokens\", \"be brief\", or invokes /caveman. Also auto-triggers when token efficiency is requested.\n- caveman:cavecrew: Decision guide for delegating to caveman-style subagents. Tells the main thread WHEN to spawn `cavecrew-investigator` (locate code), `cavecrew-builder` (1-2 file edit), or `cavecrew-reviewer` (diff review) instead of doing the work inline or using vanilla `Explore`. Subagent output is caveman-compressed so the tool-result injected back into main context is ~60% smaller \u2014 main context lasts longer across long sessions. Trigger: \"delegate to subagent\", \"use cavecrew\", \"spawn investigator/builder/reviewer\", \"save context\", \"compressed agent output\".\n- caveman:caveman-help: Quick-reference card for all caveman modes, skills, and commands. One-shot display, not a persistent mode. Trigger: /caveman-help, \"caveman help\", \"what caveman commands\", \"how do I use caveman\".\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 \u2192 4.6, 4.6 \u2192 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",
    "isInitial": true,
    "skillCount": 29,
    "type": "skill_listing"
  },
